   a8=(0,0,0,15767 = birth ms), a9=(0,1,0,0) stride 16 (corner/uv). vb in 0xC7../0xCF.. (RSX local).
   VS: age = c462.x - a8.w, life c463.x, colour keys c436/c437+, out o0 pos, o8 uv, o10 colour.
   => the draw is submitted; it's invisible by its VS/FP result (age/colour/size/pos) or a backend skip.

## >>> RELEASE 2026-10-30 <<<
dist\NHL-Legacy-Native_20261030.zip (290,211,645 B, SHA256 5A7E4001EDD2AAEFA3195AD1D70382BEAD59020EF0A43376C91EEF7D4027589E; same file as dist\NHL-Legacy-Native.zip): game 657562A6907A... (= nhl\build #267 incl. #259-#266), launcher 68C91EDEAECF..., README with measured SYSTEM REQUIREMENTS (6C min 3.8 GHz Ryzen 7000 / Intel 12th gen+, 4C 4.0 GHz+, recommended 4.5 GHz+) and the new low-end settings. No game files. Verified: frame_cap = 30 through the ini (--config copy of install_test's ini, hz60=1) -> "[gcm] #262 one flip per 4 vblanks". Not verified: matches below 720p.
IDENTIFIED FROM STRINGS IN THE EBOOT (2026-10-30, scratchpad spustr.py): 01DBF980 = eacgfx_base_default_edge_job.elf + eacgfx_base_command_dispatch_job.elf; 01EA4600 = nhl_crowd_instanced_dispatch.elf + nhlrenderjob-skinmatrixbufferjob_spu.elf. BOTH contain Sony EDGE GEOMETRY (edgegeom.cpp / _compress / _decompress; skinning, blend shapes, culling, vertex/index decompression, output vertex/index DMA). => the biggest CPU cost (01DBF980, ~1 CPU in matches) is Edge VERTEX processing for the renderer = classic GPU work (vertex-shader skinning/decompression), NOT game logic -- corrects the gpu1 conclusion. GPU path = HLE the Edge geometry job: parse its input (segments, compressed streams, skin matrices) and do skinning/decompression in a compute or vertex shader, skipping the SPU job. Big but the one lever that could get 8th/10th gen to 30. Crowd = 01EA4600: live play 2.5-8.5% of spu1-3 -> "crowd animation off" ~3-8% in play, much more in crowd shots.
EDGE GPU PROJECT (user go, 2026-10-30 evening: "moving the other part to gpu" = Edge, the agreed order is recon -> native CPU -> GPU). Static + edge1 (#268 NHL_EDGE_LOG=1, full clock, user: live play + a stoppage + a replay; 0 errors):
 - 01DBF980 is ONLY Sony Edge (49 KB). Job entry LS 0x16B8: r3 = EA of a table of 0x180-byte records, r4 = count, fetched in batches of 5, 0x968 prefetches inputs, 0x1D0 = one segment. Order in 0x1D0: 0x22F0 init (ctx at sp+176; rec+0 = EdgeGeomSpuConfigInfo, rec+0x60 viewport info, rec+0xE0 local-to-world 3x4), 0xA048 decompress, 0xA0F8 skin (rec+0x11C != 0), 0x7010, 0x5C08 cull (r3 = triangles left; 0 -> 0x8E8 = empty hole), 0x2DB0 ring alloc (rec+0x114 = ring info EA, per SPU id from LS 0x3FFF0), 0x2A00 index out, 0x4C70 compress, 0x2BC0 vertex out, 0x30B0 + 0x3398 command hole (rec+0x110 = hole EA in the IO command area), plus SET_INDEX_ARRAY / DRAW_INDEX_ARRAY written by hand at 0x4A8-0x840. CORRECTS gpu1: the output is RSX-only by design; PUTMAP never attributed it (it shows as other programs).
 - RECORD: +0 cfg (flags 83, hole 0A-10, in FF custom, out 00/01, delta FF, idx+skin 22/23, mtx 00), +8 verts u16, +A idx u16, +10.. input EAs + sizes (main heap 4A1xxxxx), +60 viewport (w,h at +64), +70 view-proj 4x4, +B0 scales, +C0 offsets, +E0 local-to-world, +110 hole EA, +114 ring info 02329A80, +118 ?, +11C skin matrices EA (IO 4D1-4D2xxxxx = RSX-visible, written by 01EA2100 = skin matrix buffer job; heap 405Fxxxx for skin flavor 3), +120 ?.
 - FORMAT MIX IS NARROW: 18 keys are only hole size x out 00/01 x skin flavor 2/3, no blend/delta, always custom input streams. Out 00 = position only (depth/shadow passes), 01 = position + one packed attribute.
 - VOLUME (match): ~6.6M verts/s, ~27M indices/s, ~35k segments/s, ~180 verts/segment. Output ring = VRAM 0xCF400000-0xCFCFFFFF: vtx ~100 MB/s, idx ~25 MB/s.
 - STAGE COST (t=119, of spu1-3 cycles; "holeend" just means Edge has finished, ignore it): Edge total 26.9% = 0.8 CPU at full clock. A0F8 skin 34%, decomp 24%, cull 19%, fetch 4%, compress 5%, 7010 4%, vtxout 4%, alloc/idx/hole ~7%.
 - GATE BEFORE ANY REWRITE: run edgeskip1 = deck9's setup + NHL_EDGE_SKIP=1 (#268, built, title smoke x268b clean, skip itself only fires in a match; NOT yet seen in a match). Env: NHL_CPU_AFFINITY=FF NHL_SPU_ISA=avx2 GCM_FLIP_EVERY=2 HITCH=1 HITCH_NOSAMPLE=1 PS3_SCSUM=1 NHL_EDGE_SKIP=1. The user caps the CPU at ~60% (2.3 GHz) first, restores 100% after. Players, and whatever else Edge draws, will be invisible; that is expected. Compare the in-play [HZ] game counter + the user's feel with deck9 / mhz1 (2.4 GHz = game 5-6, slow motion; full speed = 30). Reaches or nears 30 -> Edge is on the critical chain, build the native version. Little change -> Edge is not the limiter, stop the project. Overstates the gain: it also removes those draws from the RSX side, which a GPU path would keep.
 - edgeskip1 RESULT (2026-10-30, user capped frequency at 2.3 GHz, FF + avx2 + flip 2, NHL_EDGE_SKIP=1; 133 s, 0 errors): in-play game counter 4-5 (deck9 3-4), presents 30, still [PACE] update shown x5+ = NOT full speed at 2.3 GHz. So removing Edge gives less than ~1.7x. CAUTION: this does not settle 1.3-1.5x: mhz1 shows the counter sits at 5-7 from 2.4 to 3.7 GHz and only jumps to 30 near 4.0, so it is a step function. The question is how far skip moves that cliff. Test: skip on, step the cap through 2.6 / 3.0 / 3.4 GHz in one run. Skip firing not yet confirmed by the user (were players invisible?).
 - edgeskip2 RESULT (2026-10-30, as edgeskip1, user stepped the cap, effective clock logged per second by scratchpad clocklog.ps1 -> nhl\runs\edgeskip2.clock.csv; 195 s, 0 errors; reached 1.76 / 2.36 / 3.03 GHz, no 3.4 step). In-match seconds (game median, % of seconds at 30, JM waits median), skip vs mhz1/mhz2 baseline: 1.8 GHz 5, 5%, 150 vs 1.7 GHz 4, 0%, 121 | 2.4 GHz 6, 12%, 171 vs 5, 1%, 161 | 3.0 GHz 9, 17%, 186 vs 3.1 GHz 6, 0%, 166 and 3.7 GHz 7, 4%, 180. Baseline at 4.0 GHz = 30, 86%, 177.
   (SUPERSEDED, see speed1) A first decision stopped the project on the rule "solid game 30 at 3.0 GHz". That rule used the [HZ] game counter, which turned out not to measure speed.
   THE CLIFF WAS A METRIC ARTEFACT (2026-10-30): [HZ] "game" counts top-level main ticks, not sim steps. func_000148F0 runs N steps per tick, N = free slots of the 11-slot pipeline (func_014C8078 -> sched+0xC, pacer vtbl slot 2 func_014C35AC = +0x10 - +0xC - 1). Keeping up: N = 2, game 30, steps 60. Slightly behind: N jumps to ~10, game 5-6, while steps barely change. #269 adds `steps` to [HZ] (hook at func_014C80F4, the end of each step): FULL SPEED = 60 steps/s in a match (the title also runs 60). JM waits are ~3.0-3.2 per step, so JM/3 estimates steps for older runs in the same scene.
   speed1 RESULT (2026-10-30, Edge running, FF + avx2 + flip 2, user stepped the cap; 0 errors): real steps/s avg by clock: 1.5 GHz 38 (63%) | 2.4 GHz 46 (77%) | 3.0 GHz 54 (90%) | 3.6 GHz 58.5 (97%) | 4.0 GHz 60 (100%). USER: "3.4+ felt playable, 3.4 had some slowdowns, 3.7 was smooth". The steps counter agrees with the feel; game did not (game 5-7 at 3.6 GHz while the user called 3.7 smooth). NO CLIFF: speed falls gradually.
   CONSEQUENCES: (1) the "need ~4.0 GHz" min spec, the mhz1-5 conclusions and README SYSTEM REQUIREMENTS are too strict: a 4C/8T Zen 5-class CPU is smooth at ~3.7 GHz, playable at 3.4, and 90% speed at 3.0. (2) "1.3-1.5x needed for 8th/10th gen" came from that false line, so the real need is smaller. (3) Edge re-read with JM/3: edgeskip2 ~50 / 57 / 62 steps at 1.8 / 2.4 / 3.0 GHz vs baseline ~41 / 46 / 54 -> Edge skipped is about 1.15-1.25x real speed and about full speed at 3.0 GHz = the go condition is probably MET after all. Estimate only (scene spread is large: edgeskip1 134 JM vs edgeskip2 171 at similar clocks), and still an upper bound (the draws vanish).
   core1 RESULT (2026-10-30, user: "why do 4 cores lose so much vs 6 and 6 vs 8"; one match at effective 1.5 GHz, scratchpad coresweep.ps1 cycles the live process affinity every 15 s, env NHL_SPU_PRIO=0 NHL_SPU_CORES=0 avx2 flip 2; nhl\runs\core1.core.csv; 0 errors): speed / logical CPUs used: 4C/4T 40% / 3.83 (7 s only) | 4C/8T 58% / 6.77 | 6C/6T 62% / 5.41 | 6C/12T 66% / 7.01 | 8C/8T 74% / 6.35 | 8C/16T 63% / 7.17. SMT ON IS SLOWER THAN OFF AT 8 CORES: threads share physical cores while others idle. 8C/8T leaves ~1.6 cores free and is still 74% -> the rest of the loss at 1.5 GHz is the critical chain.
   SPIN FOUND (#270): sys_timer_usleep below 1 ms is a QPC + SwitchToThread busy-wait, and render thread tid 7 polls its fence with usleep(250), tid 5 also polls (sc141@00C774E8). Title: t7 99% of a logical CPU. NHL_PLACE_FILE (live per-thread affinity, token usleep=1 = g_usleep_block = high-resolution waitable timer; [CPU] per-role % from thread cycle counters) -> title t7 2%, same 30 presents / 60 steps.
   place1 RESULT (2026-10-30, effective 1.5 GHz, one match, 2 rounds of 15 s each; 0 errors): speed / CPUs used: 4C/8T 55% / 7.05 -> usleep 57% / 6.68 -> usleep + hot threads placed (main, s1-3 alone on even CPUs, the rest on odd) 59% / 5.92 | 6C/12T 71% / 6.92 -> usleep 81% / 5.99 | 8C/16T 70% / 7.02 -> usleep 75% / 6.06 | 8C no SMT + usleep 74% / 5.79. t7 79-89% -> 43-53%, t5 16-35% -> 4%. Still busy: s1-3 73-87%, s0/4/5 56-75%, rsx 42-63%. 23 s per layout, scene noise maybe +/-5%. NOT shipped: g_usleep_block default is off.
   place2 RESULT (2026-10-30, effective 1.5 GHz, usleep sleeping in all layouts, live SPU poll back-off; 0 errors): speed / CPUs: 4C/8T default (N=64, 100 us) 62% / 6.46 | N=8, 200 us 65% / 6.24 | N=2, 500 us 35% / 2.52 || 6C/12T default 72% / 6.13 | N=8 72% / 6.05 | N=2 36% / 2.46. s0/4/5 stay 61-70% busy at N=8, so they are NOT idle-polling at LS 0x3EE00: that CPU is real work (or some other wait, unproven). N=2 halves the game: job pickup delay is on the critical chain, so do not lengthen that back-off.
   ANSWER TO "why do fewer cores lose": the game has ~6 logical CPUs of real demand. Averages of core1/place1/place2: 4C/8T 56% -> 60% with the usleep fix; 6C/12T 68% -> 76%; 8C/16T 66% -> 75%. 4C/8T gives ~5 core-equivalents, so it is short of capacity. 6 and 8 cores are EQUAL within noise: nothing is lost from 8 to 6; the rest at this clock is the critical chain (main waits ~650 ms/s on jobs). The earlier 6C vs 4C gap in README stays valid. 4C placement (+3%) is within noise, not adopted.

USER: "felt fine no differences". Not checked: hz60 mode, a real older CPU, Proton.

So the user's "3.4 had some slowdowns" = effective 3.0 GHz (90%), "3.7 smooth" = effective 3.6 GHz (97%). Always use the logged clock.
   6-CORE RE-READ (mhz3-5 had no steps counter; JM waits / 3, so estimates): 6C/12T 1.8 GHz ~81% | 2.4 GHz ~95% | 2.9 GHz ~98% | 3.4 GHz+ ~100%. 4C/8T measured 77% at 2.4 GHz. => CORRECTS "the limit is single-thread speed, not core count": going from 4 to 6 cores gains more than Edge would. dist\NHL Legacy Native\README.txt requirements rewritten to match; ZIP NOT REBUILT (still the old README + hash 5A7E4001...; do it at the next release, the dev exe also has #268/#269).

A native version that is 2-3x faster on Edge keeps roughly half to two thirds of that (~1.08-1.11x, estimate). The game counter stays in [HZ] but must not be used as a speed metric.

## >>> PERF TECHNIQUES RESEARCH (user asked, 2026-10-30) <<<
Web research vs this port, checked on the real build (ppu_recomp_*.cpp.obj, llvm-objdump). Nothing below is built yet; speed gains are estimates.
 1. PPU MEMORY GUARDS (biggest, not in any other recomp): each lifted load runs vm_inl_ok (vm_inline_ok, range, inject_base + 0x2008, ppu_vm_size), each store also vm_store_inline_ok, g_barrier_sync_watch, g_null_sweep_hi, g_gcm_sema_watch_ea, g_spu_coh_armed + bitmap (ppu_recomp.h vm_inl_ok / vm_st_ok). 6 TUs: 2.94M x86, ~49.6k accesses (~26k loads, ~23.5k stores); roughly 13 / 24 instrs of checks each = ~25-30% of static lifted code (runtime share unmeasured). XenonRecomp: a plain swapped access. Idea: one flag / per-64 KB page flag table replaces the chain, slow path keeps all the semantics. Also MOVBE almost never fires (1,514 in 615k in one TU) because atomic load + bswap does not fold.
 2. PPU FLAGS + REGISTERS (XenonRecomp non_volatile_as_local, cr_as_local, xer_as_local, ctr_as_local; Unleashed: "several ms" of frame time, exe -20 MB). Here every register is in ctx->gpr[], every addic (SN compiler uses it as a plain add) writes ctx->xer, every compare writes ctx->cr. ctx traffic 9.6% of x86; xer + cr writes est ~15% (needs liveness in the lifter). Full PPU recompile ~10 min.
 3. SPU ATOMICS: RPCS3 PUTLLC16 (a third of SPU atomic loops touch only 16 bytes -> host cmpxchg16b, helps non-TSX CPUs). Ours serialises through one global spu_lockline_lock with spin + SwitchToThread after 4096 pauses; lock-holder preemption is a likely 4-core cost, unmeasured. Measure lock wait first.
 4. RPCS3 event-based SPU waits (self-notifying waiters, OS sleep in detected loops) instead of timed back-off: only useful if real spin is left. place2 says the LS 0x3EE00 poll is not it; s0/4/5's 60%+ busy is still unexplained -> sample them first.
 5. RENDERER per-draw costs (plan step 2 above; Unleashed skips needless copies, bindless): FP hash ~6.7%, constants ~4%, texture checksums ~3% of the ticker. Counts for 4 cores now that they are short of total capacity.
 6. PGO refresh: release checklist already; nhl.profdata is 10-24 and the AVX2 SPU copy has no profile at all. BOLT: per Google's test little on top of good PGO, and I believe ELF-only, not Windows PE.
 USER 2026-10-30: do 1-3, then decide on 4 and 5.  ITEM 3 DECIDED: no lock rework. lockline wait 4-5% of ONE CPU in a match (contended ~7%). BUT lockline runs ~3 MILLION times a second (title 37k/s; Edge is only 35k segments/s), far more than the job traffic explains. Likely GETLLAR polling that never reaches SPU_LLAR_BACKOFF (needs 32 unchanged GETLLARs in a row on the SAME EA, so a poll over 2+ lines resets it every time, the same shape SPU_POLL_LSA's comment describes) -> strong lead for item 4 and for why s0/4/5 stay 60%+ busy. NEXT: histogram lockline by EA / caller.

Back-off slept 0 times. Most of the lock traffic is PPU stores to lines with a sticky reservation mark (#153). Open: do those stores ever find a holder? #273 adds [COHLINE].  SPU AUDIT (2026-10-30, 01DBF980 AVX2 object): stack traffic 23% of the program, 40% of A7C8. Hot loops are single-function self loops, but the code around them is chopped: A71C -> A7C8 -> A9A8 -> A9E8 -> AB6C -> ACC4 -> AFBC -> A5CC, each hop a write-back of 60-70 vectors, Win64 save of xmm6-15 + 8 GPRs, __chkstk (5 KB frame), 60-70 reloads. 876 TLS guards (the SPU build lacked /Zc:tlsGuards-). --merge-chunks (#144) and --call-summaries fix the hops but were left opt-in because of play200 / play201 "4-8 / 6-15 updates/s" = the broken game counter, with the SPUs not saturated. RETEST with [HZ] steps. Other ideas: preserve_none calling convention for lifted functions (no xmm/GPR saves at all), constant shufb control vectors.
 #275 BUILT (NHL_SPU_MERGE=1 relift + /Zc:tlsGuards- for SPU; originals in nhl\spu_pre275, exe before it NHLLegacy_274.exe): SPU source 125 -> 321 MB, full build 18 min, exe 415 -> 704 MB (standalone chunk + merged copy of everything). 01DBF980 AVX2 x86 148k -> 330k; 0 TLS guards left. Merged 0A120 = 44,882 x86 with 39% stack traffic: size alone says nothing, only `steps` decides. Smoke x275 clean, title 60 steps, 0 errors. MERGE REJECTED for good reason now, measured with steps; the #144 decision stands. Cause not investigated (2.2x more code, more spills). --call-summaries not retested. Restored nhl\spu_pre275, /Zc:tlsGuards- stays. Restoring files needs a mtime touch or ninja keeps the merged objects.
 WHY MERGE IS SLOWER (user asked before the revert; merged exe kept as nhl\build\NHLLegacy_275merged.exe + .map): the loops got BETTER: A7C8 loop 1,509 x86, 27% stack, 0 calls vs the 2,762 x86, 41% stack, 65 calls chunk. The cost is at calls: a merged function promotes every register of the group, so each call writes back and reloads all of them: 0A120 208 moves per call (chunked at most 62), 01D0 174, 5C18 190, even around tiny helpers (1960, 1978, 19A0, 19A8, 19B8). --call-summaries: 208 -> 3 for those helpers, 157 for 2030, 208 for B1B0 (the assert printer, cold), but 01D0 stays 174 for its large callees. The two flags were only ever tested apart. TEST: NHL_SPU_MERGE_ONLY (merge + summaries for named lifts) on 01DBF980 + 01EA4600 only, keeps size and build time down. Whether that beats chunked is unknown until a match. NOTE: an earlier note here says "cause not investigated"; this replaces it.

 CRITICAL PATH (2026-10-30, tl1-3, all 8 cores at effective 1.5 GHz, 54 steps = 90%; 0 errors): main 33% running, 65% in the JM wait (~70 waits/s; a step is ~6 ms main + ~12 ms waiting). During waits: nobody working 0.4%, median 6 workers active, <= 2 workers only 12%. NO SERIAL CHOKEPOINT and no wake-up latency problem. SPU time per s: 01DBF980 0.90 | 01E80B00 0.46 | 01DB3300 0.40 | 01E26780 0.20 | job 01DE9800 0.20 | 01EA4600 0.14, rest flat. Edge runs on slots 1-3, 01DB3300 on 4-5. 01DB3300, 01E26780, 01DE9800 only ever run on one SPU at a time. 01E80B00 is what most often finishes last (52% of long waits) but 45% of it is its DMA loop at 0x493C (same routine as 01DB3300's 0x52AC), so it is a data mover, not an algorithm. t4 runs in 54% of waits but 3% at the end.
 CHANNEL COST (tl3, % of one CPU): GET 1.3M/s 1.4 GB/s 29% (~215 ns each) | PUT 200k/s 400 MB/s 19% (~950 ns each) | atomic 600k/s 17% | other channel writes 3.7M/s 10% (~65 ns each) = ~75%, inside it lock wait 12%. Poll sleeps 150-190% are idle time. rdch not measured. Timeline attribution rule: pc inside the loaded program first, the resident table keeps stale jobs.
 => The "big thing" is not one thing; the widest shared cost is the SPU channel/DMA runtime, which every job pays. NEXT: audit that path like the PPU memory checks.
 prof1 (2026-10-30, HITCH_SPU, 8 cores at 1.5 GHz; 0 errors): of SPU thread samples: poll sleep 18%, channel/DMA runtime ~9% (mfc_do_transfer 1.6, spu_overlay_note_get 1.0, spu_wrch_body 0.9, lockline 0.9, spu_lookup 0.6, gcm_note_spu_put 0.6), memcpy 1.2%, lifted code ~70% and very flat (hottest function 1.4%). So the runtime is worth maybe 2-3% if halved. THE BIG THING IS THE COST OF TRANSLATED SPU CODE IN GENERAL: A7C8 ~110 SPU instructions per pass -> at least 1,500 x86.
 IDEA (AVX2 only, Deck + older CPUs): set MXCSR round-toward-zero on SPU threads and use native vmulps / vaddps / vfmadd + a small |r| >= FLT_MAX check, ~6 instead of ~12-20 instructions per op (lean float is ~38% of the A7C8 loop). Needs: exact paths and TwoSum run in round-to-nearest (switch MXCSR in the slow path), check SPU double ops and conversions, interpreter, runtime code on those threads; verify with test_spu_float_lean. ceil1 = speed3 setup without NHL_SPU_ISA=avx2 measures the ceiling (AVX-512 path on 4 cores).

## >>> EDGE GPU PORT -- PARKED 2026-10-31 (native CPU #292 kept, see GPU PORT PARKED below), was BUILDING (user: "lets build it", 2026-10-31) <<<
Goal: player geometry (Sony Edge job, SPU program 01DBF980, ~17% of Deck CPU) done by the GPU instead of the SPU threads. Recon facts are in EDGE GPU PROJECT at the top of this file. Measured before the go: deck4real (all Edge work skipped) ~+10% on the Deck; the user chose to build it anyway, and the build is the real test.
DESIGN: the SPU job keeps its cheap parts (record fetch, ring alloc, command hole, completion), so the guest's command stream and job sync are untouched. It skips decompress / skin / cull / compress / vertex+index output (CPU), and records a GPU work item (input stream EAs+sizes, skin matrix EA, formats, output ring offsets). The D3D12 backend sees a draw whose vertex/index arrays lie in the Edge ring (VRAM 0xCF400000-0xCFCFFFFF), runs the queued work as ONE batched compute dispatch (decompress + skin + output format) before the draw, and draws from that GPU buffer instead of uploading guest bytes. Culling moves to the rasterizer (cull state from the Edge config), so output vertex/index counts are known up front (verts = input verts, indices = input indices).
STEPS:
 0. CAPTURE (#291, built, smoke x291 clean): NHL_EDGE_DUMP=<dir> writes ~240 segments (12 per format key) with the record, regs + full LS at every stage entry and every output PUT -> nhl\runs\edgedump1. NEEDS: one short PC match (user drives; ~1 min of play is enough).
 1. C REFERENCE (offline, no user): rebuild decompress (A048), skin (A0F8, flavors 2/3), output compress (4C70) and index decode from the lifted code; check against the corpus stage by stage (byte-exact, or float-ulp for skinned positions).
 2. HLSL compute: port the C reference; offline D3D12 harness compares GPU vs C on the corpus.
 3. INTEGRATION behind NHL_EDGE_GPU=1: SPU-side skip + work queue; backend ring-draw detection + batched dispatch; title smoke, then a PC match (user: visuals, players/shadows), then the Deck.
 4. Deck match vs deck13real / deck11real (steps).
 STEP 0 DONE (2026-10-31, user drove the capture match): nhl\runs\edgedump1 = 216 segment files (655 MB), 0 bad, all 18 format keys; SNAPs at stages 0 + 2..12 (216 each), PUTs 216 index + 170 vertex. Reader: scratchpad edge\corpus.py <dir> [index].
 TOOLS MOVED: bench + corpus tools now live in _port_work\edge (bench\build.ps1, bench\harness.c, corpus.py, recdump.py, descs.py, edge.dis, edge_ref.c/.h, ref_test.c).
 FORMAT FACTS (2026-10-31, all 216 corpus segments): ONE input layout: stride 12: pos 3 x BE u16 fixed 2.14 (+ per-segment 3 x BE s32 offset, then / 2^14), normal + tangent format 9 = 3 bytes: X 10 bits, Y 10 bits, 4 bits idx: idx>>2 = axis of the dropped (largest) component, idx&1 = its sign (0 = negative), idx&2 = w. X/Y -> k*c1 + c0 (+-sqrt(1/2)); SPU does this with a FUSED fma on 1.0 + k*2^-23: unfused math rounds to 2^-10, so HLSL/C must use k*c1+c0. LS layout: C300 indexes, then palette, weights (8 B/vtx: w0 i0 w1 i1 w2 i2 w3 i3, w/255), vertexes, fixed offsets, stream desc (ctx+0x6C). Output format 0 = pos F32c3 (stride 12), 1 = pos + normal + tangent X11Y11Z10N (stride 20; floor((v*(1-2^-11) - 2^-11)*1024), z: (1-2^-10), 2^-10, *512). Skin flavor 2: normal + tangent = blended 3x3, normalized; flavor 3: normal = cofactor of blended 3x3, tangent = 3x3, normalized. Blend = sum w_i M_i (BE 3x4 row-major, 0x30 each). Table 4 = skin / cull scratch only. Stage glue: no return values used; ctx+0x54/58/5C/60/64/160 are the only state.
 STEP 1 C REFERENCE DONE (2026-10-31, edge\edge_ref.c, edge\ref_test.c): all 216 segments, 137,047 verts: pos max abs err 5.7e-6, normal/tangent max err 0.00015 (SPU's estimated sqrt), packed words 94.5% exact, the rest 1 LSB, none worse.
 #292 NATIVE CPU STAGES (2026-10-31; order agreed at the top of this file: recon -> native CPU -> GPU; nhl\port\nhl_edge_native.c, NHL_EDGE_NATIVE=1, hooks in nhl_hooks.py): AVX2 decompress + skin + compress, on the lifted cull/index/alloc/hole. Falls back to lifted + logs [EDGE-NATIVE] for any other layout. Bench (edge\bench, NHL_EDGE_NATIVE=1): 216 segs: ctx bytes bad 0, pos max err 7.6e-6, packed 94.5% exact / rest 1 LSB, lifted cull on native positions differs in 4 of 216 segments (tiny rounding on borderline triangles, not yet looked at). COST 429 -> 19 kcyc/seg (22x) on the PC, ~30 cycles/vertex. Lifted cull is now 149 of ~194 kcyc left. Regression with the env off: 0 LS bytes differ.
 #292 NATIVE CULL ADDED (2026-10-31): func 0x5C08 + its vertex pass 0x5850 in nhl_edge_native.c. Decoded: M = viewproj (ctx+E0) * world 3x4 (ctx+A0); sx/sy/sz = xyz*|1/w|*scale (ctx+120) + offset (ctx+130); vertex flags = outside x+ x- y+ y- z+ z- <<7..2, w>0 <<1 (viewport ctx+D0); sample step 2.0 for aa modes 3/4 (byte ctx+140), else 1.0. Triangle dropped if AND of flags & 0xFC, or, when all w>0 and none below z min: all three round to the same x or y column (trunc(s*step+0.5)), or backface: area = (Bx-Ax)(Cy-Ay) - (By-Ay)(Cx-Ax), sign flipped when index format (ctx[6]>>4) is 0 or 2 XOR mode 3, cull if > 0, mode 1 has no backface test. Index list compacted in place, ctx+190 = new index count, free pointer = list end rounded to 16, r3 = count. Stays lifted: mode 0 (nothing to do, 4 corpus segments), aa 5 (second SPU loop, not in the corpus), index bias, custom transform.
 RESULT: cullsim.py, these rules on the SPU's own vertex records: 0 wrong triangles in 212 culled segments, so the rules are exact. Bench with native positions: 5 of 216 segments differ, one triangle each; with the SPU's positions 2 of 216 (rounding of its estimated 1/w, borderline triangles). Scratch table poisoned before stages 7-12 (BENCH_POISON=1): no change, nothing else reads it. Env off: stages 3/4/9 0 bytes, stage 6 the known 26 B of stack. COST kcyc/seg: skin all-in 18, index 9, cull 157 -> 11, alloc 1, idx 2, compress 1, vtx out 11, hole 2: whole job ~612 -> ~55 (11x, PC). Index decode and vtx out are now about 35% of what's left.

 - ICF / dead-code strip: already on (no /DEBUG -> lld-link defaults /OPT:REF,ICF; /O2 implies /Gy).
 - Huge pages: Deck THP is already [always].
 - Compiler flags on the hottest AVX2 SPU TU (nhl_prog_01DBF980, 369 Deck-hot fns = 17% of Deck cycles), weighted instr: base 766, no-PGO 753, -Os 753, -mtune=znver2 762, stack refs 241-249; -fstrict-aliasing = byte-identical. Nothing to gain.
 - RPCS3 #18395 (the 2026 SPU "breakthrough": loop iteration prediction + LS/context noalias, 5-7% in Twisted Metal, partly reverted later in #18979/#19050): its noalias/loop-reuse parts don't apply here; our SPU GPRs are already SSA locals.
 - AVX2 SPU copies DO get a PGO profile (same hash, ~28x lower counts than the AVX-512 copies but hot blocks still above the 99% hot threshold).
 FOUND (with numbers):
 1. PPU VMX ops are lifted as scalar byte loops (ppu_lifter.py:2929 vperm template; vadduwm nested byte loops; vcmpequw/gtuw via ppu_vmx.h per-lane bswap). Lifted PPU fns containing them = 4.6% of Deck cycles; func_00C77328 (vector scan loop, ppu_recomp_110.cpp:36806, NOT a spin-wait) alone = 1.25%. TEST (vperm\loop.c, verbatim lifted inner loop vs SSE pshufb/blendv/cmpeq/bswap helpers, same storage, identical ctx after 4096 iters): PC 15.0 -> 4.7 ns/iter (3.2x), DECK 27.7 -> 7.0 ns/iter (3.9x). Single vperm on the Deck: 29-35 -> 3.5 ns (8-10x). Est. +1-2.5% Deck (more if tid 8331 is on the critical path). Needs a lifter template change + full PPU regenerate + conformance run (scratch\ppu_conformance.cpp).
 2. mfc_do_transfer (spu_dma.h:284-910) runs ~25 getenv-gated diag checks per DMA (~12 KB of code). One "any diag on" flag + a lean path is est. +1-2%.
 3. RSX thread (tid 8308, 9.1%): rsx_fp_code_hash (5.6% of the thread) is a serial byte-FNV and rsx_fp_program_size (3.0%) walks the same ucode again; tex_csum_sampled 5.8% + tex_csum 4.3%. A fused 4-lane hash+size walk is est. 0.5-1% of Deck cycles. CHECK FIRST whether the FP hash keys the on-disk shader cache (a change would force a rebuild).
 4. Wine heap places the six 264 KB spu_context allocs (lv2_register.c:1109 calloc) in PAIRS at identical addresses mod 1 MB (Deck test heaptest: ctx0=ctx3, ctx1=ctx4, ctx2=ctx5) -> paired SPU threads hit the same L1/L2 sets; the PC heap scatters them. Fix = per-context offset (e.g. +i*4 KB+i*64 B). Est. 0-2%, Deck test only.
 5. Deck APU power: in matches the APU sits at 14-15.6 W and the CPU at 3.2-3.28 GHz (menus 3.5 GHz). Lower GPU load (render scale 480p, or a fixed lower GPU clock in the Deck perf overlay) may give the CPU clock back, up to ~+7%. NO CODE; needs a user Deck run vs deck10real 40.8 steps.
 6. PGO mislabel: clang gives some hot runtime fns entry count 0 despite a matching record (spu_indirect_branch 54e9 calls) -> they land in .text$unlikely (also spu_drain_call, spu_overlay_note_get, rsx_process_method, ps3_indirect_call_impl, vp_record_cb, render_frame). Root cause unknown (toy tests: >2^32 counts work). __attribute__((hot)) fixes placement only (same code). Est. <= 1%.
 7. Low-end option idea: Edge position-only (shadow/depth) output is ~26% of Edge verts; a "no player shadow casters" low-end option would save ~2-3% on the Deck (visual cost: no player shadows).
 - "[anon]" 4.9% of Deck cycles = vcruntime140 memcpy (Wine maps DLL code into anonymous memory), mostly SPU DMA + RSX. Shrinks with items 2/3.
 Suggested order: 5 (free test) -> one Deck candidate build with 1+2+3+4 -> 6/7 if wanted.

CORRECTION to item 2: perfrec_deck6real is the #282 exe; 49% of mfc_do_transfer's samples there were the SPU_POISON_DMA scan #284 already removed, so #288 is worth ~0.3-0.5%, not 1-2%. NOTE: #290 changes ~80k sites, so those PPU functions run without PGO data until the next PGO pass (release checklist). Item 5 = no-code Deck test: render_scale has no value below 1 (nhl_config.cpp: 1/1.5/2/3), so the knob is the Deck QAM performance tab "Manual GPU Clock" (e.g. 800-1000 MHz), compare steps + CPU MHz vs deck10real 40.8.

 USER 2026-10-31: no RPCS3 comparison wanted; asked for a high-risk "hail mary" for weak CPUs. PROPOSED: HALF-RATE SIM = run the 60 Hz fixed sim step (func_000148F0 loop, pipeline sched func_014C8078/80F4, step count at sched+0x84) at 30 Hz with a doubled timestep, ideally only while behind. ~3 JM waits per step -> SPU work is mostly per step, so up to ~2x on weak CPUs. Risks: physics/puck/collision accuracy, animation, game feel; dt may be a hard-coded 1/60 in many places. First step = RE where the step timestep comes from (no play needed). Alternative: SPU translation overhaul (whole-function registers, cheaper LS/channel ops), est. 1.2-1.5x, weeks, merge attempt tied.
 HALF-RATE SIM RE, day 1 (2026-10-31, user: "Go for it"; #281 diag knobs, exe rebuilt, old exe = nhl\build\NHLLegacy_280pgo.exe):
  - No f32/f64 1/60 literal in the EBOOT data (scratchpad find_dt.py; the text-segment "hits" are instruction bytes). dt is computed at run time.
  - NHL_FSCAN=<sec> (+HITCH=1) scans guest memory for 1/60: at the title, 80 hits = 16 records at 024F3000 stride 0x3C0 (+0: 4x dt splat, +0x10: 4x 1.0 (time scale?), +0x28: dt, +0x2C: 5, then identity matrices and -1e8 bounds -> look like render/scene view contexts, not the sim), 3 at 025252C0 stride 0x14. NHL_FDUMP=<ea>,<len> dumps a record. Code using 0x24F base: ppu_recomp_024 (~20 sites), func_01A26A80 writes 0x24F191C/1920.
  - NHL_STEPPROBE=1 (hand probe in ppu_recomp_204 func_014C80F4, marked [NHL-STEPPROBE], temp): the per-step call is func_01A26A5C(obj 4D029D20) -> func_014C33CC = frame-PIPELINE slot bookkeeping (atomic counter, ring index mod count, +0x28 frame number). So a "step" is a slot of an 11-slot multi-stage frame pipeline, not one physics call; the sim work runs in the stages (JM jobs) fed per slot.
  - NEXT: find the stage that advances game time (watch a match-clock float during a match, or trace who reads the 024F3000/025252C0 dt fields per step), then test 2x dt + 1 slot per tick.
 HALF-RATE SIM RE, day 1 continued (user away, title boots only):
  - Per-step loop (func_000148F0 loc_00014BA4..func_014C80F4): func_00014808(app) gate, func_00014378/000141D4 (label 0x0232FF8D, profiler-like), func_0094B188(*0x0222FF20) = virtual walk over a task list, then r31->vtbl[+0x24](r31) (r31 = *(0x0217..-0x627C), the game update) -- NO dt argument anywhere.
  - PPU_RVAL=DT + NHL_VM_GATE=0 (#281, counts reads of f32 1/60 per address): in 90 s only 3 reads, each ONCE (literal 00D90EA4 in func_00D90EB8, literal 00BC0A40 in func_00BC0A58, heap 4062A0F4 in func_0073C27C). PPU_RVAL64=3F911111: no f64 1/60 reads. PPU_WVAL=DT (#281, stores of 1/60 per writer fn): 2 stores in 80 s, both at boot (func_01A295A0 = the 024F3000 record ctor, written once; func_016C88D8). SPU code: no 1/60 immediates (ilhu 0x3C88) and no raw 1/60 words (scratchpad spu_dt.py).
  - NHL_DT2=1 (double the 16 records' dt mid-run) + NHL_FCLOCK_MUL: title clocks too noisy (ring of GPU constants at C13xxxxx), no clean sign that anything follows them.
  - Pipeline object 4D029D20: vtbl 01D72C68, +C 10, +0x10 11 slots, +0x30 frame counter; no rate field.
  - CONCLUSION: the sim has no time-step variable. It is locked to the display: one step per vblank, with per-step behaviour baked into the code (velocities, decays, frame-count timers, animation advance). A 30 Hz sim would mean re-tuning game logic across PPU and SPU code, not a patch. HALF-RATE SIM CLOSED (unless a match-time trace shows otherwise).

 deck5real RESULT (2026-10-31, live match, #280 PGO exe, low-end preset, no flip skip; nhl\runs\deck5real.pmu.txt + deck5real.perfstat.txt; user space only):
  - deckpmu 40 s: process ~17.7 G cycles/s (~5.5 CPUs at 3.24 GHz) and ~21.9 G instr/s = IPC 1.24 (busy-loop test 2.4). Per 1k instr: L1I miss 1.2 (fine), iTLB 0.03, L1D miss ~10.4 (high), dTLB 0.17, branch miss 0.8. Generic stalled-cycles-frontend ~30%.
  - Six consecutive tids 7105-7110 = the SPU threads: 2.0-2.5 G cycles/s each (~65-77% of a core in user space), IPC 1.0-1.5, L1D 9-12 MPKI. tid 7088 (most likely the rsx ticker incl. vkd3d's work) ~1.8 G cycles/s = ~0.57 CPU, IPC 1.2, front-end stall only 10%, L1D 15 MPKI. Three others 0.65-1.0 G (main / render / JM).
  - perf stat 30 s (Zen 2 events): ic_fetch_stall.any 59% of cycles (back_pressure 18%, dq_empty 1.7%). Instruction-cache fills from L2 17.3 per 1k instr, from beyond L2 (L3 / DRAM) 5.7 per 1k (fills include prefetch; demand fw32 misses only 1.2 per 1k). L2 misses for instruction fills 7.2 per 1k vs data L2 misses ~3.3 per 1k. l2_latency waiting-on-fills = 42% of cycles (overlapping).
  - READING: the Deck runs this code at IPC ~1.2 because of memory-hierarchy pressure on BOTH sides: instruction fetch (L2 misses for code are ~2x those for data; the 512 KB L2 + 4 MB L3 are small for the lifted-code footprint) and data (L1D 10 MPKI; two SPU threads with 256 KB LS each share a core's 512 KB L2 on 4C/8T). No single hotspot visible from totals. Renderer thread ~0.57 CPU of ~5.5 => a Vulkan backend (skip vkd3d) could save maybe 0.2-0.3 CPU, a few %: NOT worth it for speed.

L2 is 512 KB per core, L3 4 MB. Hottest function 2.5% (A7C8). Fits perf stat: L1I fills 17 per 1k vs demand misses 1.2 per 1k = the prefetcher drags in neighbouring (cold) lines.   - SPU TRANSLATOR REWRITE, PHASE 0 (2026-10-31, user: "hail mary, rewrite the SPU translator"): where do lifted-SPU cycles go? scratchpad spucost.py classifies every Deck-sampled x86 instruction (deck6real cycles, #282 exe) in the top 500 lifted functions (65% of lifted-SPU samples; lifted SPU = 55% of ALL Deck cycles): stack spill/reload 26.0% | integer/logic vector 15.8% | float ops + checks 9.8% | branch 7.8% | SPU reg <-> ctx at entry/exit/calls 7.4% | reg moves / lane extract 6.7% | LS memory 5.5% | scalar address math 5.3% | vpshufb 4.9% | rip constants 4.1% | other memory 3.5% | push/pop/vzeroupper 1.6% | calls 1.6% (skid caveat: samples land after the stalled instruction).
    * A7C8_avx2 (1,926 x86, 45% stack refs): the prologue loads ~70 SPU registers from ctx and stores most straight to the stack; loop-invariant constants (broadcasts) are hoisted and spilled; LS loads are lea + and + vmovdqu + vpshufb (lean, RPCS3-like). The spills come from the SPU code itself: its software-pipelined loops keep ~70 of 128 registers live, x86 AVX2 has 16. Function-boundary cost (the old "hop cost" theory) is only ~7%.
    * READING: the translator's per-op code is already reasonably tight; the biggest bucket is register pressure that any x86 translator (RPCS3's too) pays. AVX-512's 32 registers gave little beyond #280's float fix (ceil1 vs speed6), so spills are not as dear as their 26% share suggests on Zen 5; on Zen 2 unknown. Realistic ceiling of a rewrite: perhaps 10-20% of lifted-SPU time = ~5-10% overall on the Deck, NOT the 1.2-1.5x estimated earlier. Reported to the user before committing weeks.

RPCS3 Approximate re-read: FA plain add; FS clamp_smax(b); FM plain or NaN-masked; FMA/FMS/FNMS clamp_smax both factors, a zero factor -> addend; NO result range check, NO -0 fix. Ours now: fa native, fs clamps b, fm/fma/fms/fnms clamp both factors (2 int ops each: min_epi32 0x7F7FFFFF + min_epu32 0xFF7FFFFF, sign-preserving; 0*clamped = 0 naturally), no |r| >= FLT_MAX check, no spu__a2_rzfix call, no r+0. RZ + FTZ|DAZ kept. AVX-512 copy (PC) unchanged. BUILT: exe 390,312,448 B (#284 401,492,480: -11 MB). A7C8_avx2 1926 -> 941 x86 instrs, stack refs 865 (45%) -> 273 (29%), 32 calls -> 0 (fnx86.py): the checks + kept-live operands were most of the spills. Visuals: user "Looked fine". #286 KEPT (Deck build). LESSON: an inline fast path with an out-of-line fix that needs the op's INPUTS keeps them live past the op and that, not the check itself, was most of the spills (A7C8 865 -> 273 stack refs). Look for the same pattern in other SPU helpers before anything bigger.
    * SPILL HUNT on #286 (2026-10-31, user: "Yes, hunt it"; scratchpad hunt.py / spillsrc.py / objfn.py): top 80 hot lifted fns (deck6real ranking = 24.8% of all Deck cycles) have NO cold-path calls left (only spu_wrch / drain / lifted calls = real work) -> the #286 pattern was unique. Stack refs still 20-45% per fn. Spilled values (6 hottest): SPU regs loaded from ctx at entry 151 stores, LS / memory loads 98, shuffle results 75, clamp results (vpminud) 49, shufb-derived (vpsllw/vpxor/vpor) ~130, float results 32.
    * Rematerialisation experiment (one TU nhl_prog_01DBF980 avx2 compiled 4 ways, ~12 s each; weighted over its 10 hot fns): v0 #286 = x86 9939 / stack 3124; clamp per use (volatile asm barrier) 10459 / 3288; shufb control derived per use 10792 / 3353; both 11261 / 3478 => WORSE, clang's hoist/CSE already wins. No clamp at all: 9498 / 3067 (-4% x86, -2% stack) => the clamps cost <=1-2% of SPU time, keep them (NaN safety). Experiment switches removed.
    * CONCLUSION: what is left in the lifted SPU code is genuine register pressure (~70 live SPU regs vs 16 xmm), as Phase 0 said. SPU codegen work is done unless something structural changes. Remaining lever for the Deck: the release PGO pass trained on the AVX2 copy (NHL_SPU_ISA=avx2) -- the current profile never saw the _avx2 functions.
  - RESEARCH (2026-10-31, user: "look online, think outside the box", while deck9real ran):
    * spucost.py with a Win64 split: callee-saved xmm6-15/GPR saves + __chkstk = only 3.4% of lifted-SPU cycles (spills inside bodies 24.2%) -> preserve_none for lifted functions is worth ~2-3% of SPU time at most.
    * USER'S RPCS3 CONFIG for NHL (config_BLUS31540.yml): SPU XFloat Accuracy = Approximate, SPU Block Size Safe, loop detection off, Max SPURS 6, Accurate reservations on. RPCS3 Approximate (SPULLVMRecompiler.cpp, read 2026-10-31): FA = plain f32 add, no checks; FM / FMA = native op, lanes with a NaN-pattern input (SPU exponent 255) masked to 0 / to the addend, plain op when known-bits prove no NaN; FS clamps operand b to +-FLT_MAX; NO result range check anywhere. Our #280 (native RZ op + per-result |r| >= FLT_MAX check + out-of-line exact fix, ~1% of Deck cycles in spu__a2_rzfix alone) is STRICTER than what NHL runs with on RPCS3. IDEA A "fast float": AVX2 copy drops the result check / fix calls (keep RZ + FTZ/DAZ; optionally RPCS3's NaN-input masks). Removes ~4 ops + a cold call per float op (calls also add register pressure).
    * RPCS3 SHUFB: constant control (get_const_vector) -> plain shuffle / insert / byteswap fast paths; masks only become constant from constant-generating instructions (il/ila/fsmbi/cwd...). RPCS3 does NOT fold LS loads from the program image (make_load_ls is a plain load + byteswap; LQA/LQR addresses constant, data not). Ours: spu_shufb #157 = 9 ops; masks in hot loops are live-in registers (from callers' lqr loads), clang hoists 3 values per mask -> more spills. IDEA B "image constants": the lifter folds lqr/lqa from the program's read-only data (no static store to that qword in the image) into constants, so shufb masks become compile-time; with merge/summaries they reach the loops. Beyond RPCS3. Verify with SPU_LOCKCHECK lockstep. Count first how many hot shufb masks come from lqr/lqa.
    * Other RPCS3 bits: STQD elided when the register is already saved in a dominating block; fma32x4 skips the FMA when addend or a multiplicand is constant zero; BI with known targets -> switch.
    * Project Pyrrhus (Killzone 2 on ps3recomp) README: no SPU technique details; ~23 fps vs PS3 30 in a scripted run. pinyon-shift #456 (Xbox recomp survey): register locals with liveness-limited spills is their main unused lever (PPU side here; #272 already did flags).
  - NOT DONE (superseded by deck6real): per-function sample (scratchpad deckpmu\record.sh -> ~/Games/NHLLegacy/record.sh: perf record cycles / L1D miss / IC stall 20 s, aggregated per ip + exe mapping; the user had left the match). Needed to see whether the hot CODE footprint is large (=> PGO function ordering / hot-cold split) or the misses are LS / guest data (=> hardware limit).

 - Stage 2 (clock, only if stage 1 is fine): the USER sets Windows power plan "Maximum processor state" ~60% (~2.3 GHz; Zen 2 at 3.5 GHz ~ Zen 5 at ~2.2 GHz by IPC) and plays deck2 with the same env; set it back to 100% after.
 - Not covered: the Deck's 4 MB L3 (vs 32 MB), LPDDR5 shared with the GPU, the Deck GPU, Proton/vkd3d.
 - Read: [HZ] game N/s and pres, HITCH busy/slack for main (tid 1) / render (7) / job threads (4,5), SPU idle %, seconds below 20/s.
 - deck1 RESULT (2026-10-29, FF + avx2, clock 100%, 173 s, user closed; 0 GFXERR): menus/title fine (30/60). In the match (overlays + pause menu loaded) t=106-161 game 5-6/s ([PACE] every update shown x5+), presents 43-60; t=163-168 game 30; then menus 60. Main waits ~690 ms/s in cond_wait sc107@00C78544 (Job Manager). spu1-3 SATURATED (no idle leaf) in prog 01EA4600 (B668, 6E50, 6968, 8F00, C080) + 01DBF980 (A7C8, 5FB0, 8364, ACC4), all *_avx2 = the AVX2 EXACT float path; spu0/4/5 ~30% idle. Same programs/SPU group as play257's crowd stall (AVX-512, full CPU), but here it lasts the whole match window. Also seen: cold build\shadercache -> one-off fxc hitches 1-2.3 s (t=64, 81, 95), not Deck-specific.
   => Suspect #1: AVX2 exact float (~68-102 instr per fm/fma) vs AVX-512 lean. Suspect #2: 6 SPU + PPU threads on 8 logical CPUs. NEXT: deck1b = FF with AVX-512 (NHL_SPU_ISA unset) to split the two.
 - deck1b RESULT (2026-10-29, FF + AVX-512 lean, USER CAPPED THE CLOCK AT 2.30 GHz -- so two variables changed vs deck1; 102 s, user closed; 0 errors): front end 30/60 OK; in the match (5 overlay opens, no pause menu) t=77-102 game 3-5/s, presents 41-58. Now spu1-3 have 30-40% idle and spu0/4/5 ~50% (lean path at 2.3 GHz has room) -- vs deck1 saturated at full clock with AVX2 => the AVX2 exact float path costs well over 2x: CONFIRMED Deck bottleneck #1.

Main waits 616 ms/s in the Job Manager cond_wait (sc107@00C78544). => bottleneck #2 = single-thread speed of the render thread + RSX ticker.
   Not yet run: the full Deck combo (FF + avx2 + 2.3 GHz).

 - PLAN for #2 (proposed 2026-10-30, awaiting user OK):
   0. deck3 = the real Deck combo (FF + avx2 + user caps 2.3 GHz) + PERF=1, HITCH_TIDS=7 -> baseline: which of render thread / ticker / SPUs limits first now.
   1. Render thread (PPU code, every CPU): build the lifted PPU sources for x86-64-v3 instead of baseline+FMA (MOVBE byte-swap loads/stores, BMI2 rorx/shlx/andn for rlwinm masks, LZCNT for cntlzw). No CPU lost: the exe already needs AVX2 (SPU code has only AVX-512/AVX2 sets). Check offline: x86 instruction counts of hot render funcs (func_01516344, 014B77A4, 014F42A0, 014ED4C0) before/after; full PPU recompile ~10 min. Then the hot out-of-line helpers on tid 7 (vm_write*, barrier_watch_hit, spu_lockline_lock 3%).
   2. RSX ticker per-draw cost: (a) vp_record_cb copies the whole ~8 KB VP constant bank per draw -> copy only the dirty range; (b) rsx_fp_code_hash / program_size / fp_snapshot per draw -> memo on FP address + write generation; (c) texture binds (vp_upload_tex_slot + tex_csum, up to 17% at 176-300k binds/s) -> slot hash index, skip unchanged checksums; (d) QPC timing per tex bind in the launcher's default HITCH mode (small).

Match t=68-130: game 3/s for 60 s ([PACE] every update shown x5+). Busy in the match: spu1-3 ~85% (01DBF980 A7C8/8364/5FB0/5AC8 + 01EA4600 B668), render thread tid 7 ~84%, rsx-ticker ~78%, main 57% in the JM cond_wait (only 78 waits/s, max 70 ms), spu0/4/5 ~35-40% idle.

So for the Deck the per-update cost of EACH of spu1-3, tid 7 and the ticker must fit 33 ms at Deck speed: ~2-2.5x cheaper than today at full clock.    WHY: total demand ~5.2 busy logical CPUs on 4C/8T (~5 core-equivalents with SMT) = the whole CPU. spu0/4/5 "half busy" is mostly the JM kernel idle loop (s0b 3F450/3FBB0/3F0D0 on stack, 01E26780 job-runner poll at LS 0xF00/0x2200 with NO back-off; SPU_POLL_LSA covers only 0x3EE00, 64 unchanged polls before each 100 us sleep) sharing SMT cores with main/render.
   NEXT TEST (env only): deck6 = deck5 + SPU_POLL_N=8 SPU_POLL_US=200 -> idle SPUs sleep sooner/longer. Then a back-off for the 01E26780 poll.
 - deck6 RESULT (2026-10-30, deck5 + SPU_POLL_N=8 SPU_POLL_US=200; 88 s, 0 errors; USER: "still slow"): main JM waits/s in the match ~105 avg (t=73-78 ~155, then 80-90) vs deck5 114, full speed 186 -> NO gain; poll knobs stay at their defaults.
   USER (2026-10-30): keep optimising on this 4C/8T + 2.3 GHz setup ("if someone is running this computer setup ... could be even better on steam deck") -- it stands for low-end PCs too.
   Correction: 01E26780 func 23AC is the JOB RUNNER (it calls the job bodies), so it is on the stack whenever any job runs -- not an idle poll. The "~1.5 CPUs of polling" estimate was not backed; a full budget is needed first.
   #263 BUILT (boot_main.cpp): HITCH_TOP=<n> (max 120) = n leaves and n stack entries per thread, 0.1% resolution. Smoke x263 clean. NEXT: deck7 = deck5 env + HITCH_TOP=60 -> CPU budget of the match grouped by image (SPU program/job, kernel, runtime helpers, PPU guest, waits) -> attack the biggest slices.
   deck7 RESULT (2026-10-30, deck5 env + HITCH_TOP=60; 107 s, 0 errors): match t=78-106 main JM waits ~100/s (full speed 186). CPU BUDGET (scratchpad budget.py; logical CPUs over the 11 sampled threads, 4.5 of them waiting): 01DBF980 1.12, PPU guest code 0.60, spu_channels.c runtime 0.46, 01EA4600 0.26, rsx_d3d12_backend 0.17, CRT memcpy 0.16, SPU float fallback (spu__ax_fma_fix etc.) 0.15, job 01DE9800 0.14, cellGcmSys 0.13, 01DB3300 0.12, spu_coherency 0.11, ntdll 0.10, rsx_commands 0.09, rsx_fp_decompiler 0.08, ppu_loader 0.08. Per thread busy: spu1-3 83-85% (01DBF980 ~37% each: A7C8 9.7, 8364 5.4, 5FB0 4.3, 8060 3.0, ACC4 2.8, 5AC8 2.7, 21BC 2.7, 5014 2.1; 01EA4600 ~8.5; channels ~5.5; float fallback ~3.8), spu0/4/5 64-67%, ticker 64%, tid 7 64%, main 43%, tid4 21%, tid5 8% => ~6.5 busy logical CPUs on 8 = the CPU is full.
   CODEGEN: AVX2 vs AVX-512 now close (A7C8 2204 vs 2333 x86 instrs; 8364 1227 vs 1098; 5FB0 1878 vs 1764; B668 is ICF-folded with A7C8); ~30% of the instructions in both are %rsp spill traffic (register pressure: >16 live u128 values).
   #264 BUILT+TESTED: lean AVX2 float fix-up per lane (boundary lanes settled in-vector, only Inf/NaN lanes scalar-exact; fast path unchanged). test_spu_float_lean AVX2/AVX-512 96M lanes 0 failures each; controls fail (NO_DAZ 1,765,025; no-decrement 1,199,543). Expected: most of the ~3.8% fallback on spu1-3. Exe built (old NHLLegacy_263.exe kept); smokes x264b (AVX2) / x264a (AVX-512) 50 s title: 0 errors, 1518 / 1526 flips. Not yet measured in a match.
   deck8 RESULT (2026-10-30, #264 + #265, deck7 env; 123 s, 0 errors): match t=75-123 main JM waits ~113/s avg (deck7 103) = +10%. spu1 busy 74% (deck7 85%): 01DBF980 30.5%, channels 5.2%, float fallback (0206C980 obj = spu__a2_fix etc.) 2.6%. Whole process: 5.17 of the sampled threads' capacity waiting (deck7 4.50). Scenes differ between runs (01EA4600 8.5% -> 2.5% on spu1), so +/-5% is noise.
   USER Q: is the mod hurting perf / what settings are on? The dev runs (run_nhl.ps1) use nhl\hdd0 = update + the user's mod baked in (610 files, 2.2 GB: texlib/name/sock/jersey/goaliehelmet/centerlogo textures, team logos) -> mostly load time, VRAM, texture upload/checksums on the RSX ticker (not the critical path). Measurement cost: HITCH sampling suspends + unwinds 11 threads at 1 kHz (~11k samples/s) -- real CPU on 4C/8T and extra latency on the critical threads; players run HITCH_NOSAMPLE=1. So deck1-8 numbers are somewhat pessimistic. NEXT: deck9 = deck8 without sampling (HITCH_NOSAMPLE=1, keep PS3_SCSUM for the JM metric) -> the player's real speed on this setup; mod-off test only if needed (needs a mod-free hdd0 or the launcher's Mods tab).
   deck9 RESULT (2026-10-30, deck8 without sampling: HITCH_NOSAMPLE=1, PS3_SCSUM=1; 130 s, 0 errors; USER: "feels maybe a little bit faster"): match t=77-130 JM waits avg 111.5 / median 111 = deck8 (113 / 111) -> the sampler's cost is negligible; deck numbers stand. Peaks to 150-176/s for a few s (t=70-76, 114-117: lighter scenes). Sim ~60% of full speed (111/186) on 4C/8T @ 2.3 GHz with everything so far.
   RSX TICKER DETAIL (deck8, busy ~54%): rsx_process_method 7.7, fifo walk 6.2, gpu_vtx_draw 3.7, rsx_fp_code_hash 3.5, memcpy 3.2, render_frame 3.1, tex_csum 1.8, rsx_fp_program_size 1.5, vp_record_cb 1.1, rsx_fp_extract_consts 1.1, tex_csum_sampled 1.1, rsx_fetch_attrib 0.9, fp_shadow_from_draw 0.6, vp_upload_tex_slot 0.6. => FP hashing ~6.7%, constants copy ~4%, texture checksums ~3%. The ticker is NOT on the critical path: cutting a third of it frees ~0.15 of ~6.5 busy logical CPUs = est. 2-3% sim speed. FP memo risk: NV40 FP constants are embedded in the program code (rsx_fp_extract_consts), so a memo that misses a guest rewrite = wrong shader. Put to the user before building.
   SPU SPILL LOOK (2026-10-30, user picked it over the RSX work): 01DBF980 A7C8 = a hand software-pipelined SPU loop (~110 SPU instrs/iteration, 32 float ops, 27 LS loads, 8 stores, 18 shufb; ~70 live SPU registers at once -- written for 128 registers). AVX2 x86 mix (2145 instrs): ~550 register<->stack moves (vmovdqa 299, vmovaps 151, vmovapd 75, vmovdqu 25), ~570 lean float path (~20 per fma: 3 cvtps2pd + mulpd + TwoSum 5 + edge/big checks + narrow + addps), ~110 LS access (lea/and/load/pshufb), 38 vbroadcastsd constant re-loads, 46 cold slow-path calls. The spills are inherent (70 live values vs 16 ymm). Possible: (a) compute TwoSum only when a lane's sum lands on a 24-bit grid point (branch; ~5% of the routine, mispredict risk); (b) lifter float-type tracking to keep chained float values widened in double (skip cvt pairs, ~7%; big lifter change). Est. total 5-15% of the SPU threads -> ~5-10% sim on this setup. Full speed (1.7x) at 4C/8T @ 2.3 GHz looks out of reach with incremental work.
   GPU OFFLOAD QUESTION (user: "anything else we can push to the gpu?"; run gpu1 2026-10-30, #266 SPU_PUTMAP, full clock, match t~70-118): per 10 s in the match -- 01E26780 (job runner; it DMAs each job's output) 2.28 GB into main heap 42A00000 (1.4 GB), 43000000-43300000, 42C00000 (va-draws 0); 01E80B00 ~1 GB heap 48E-4BA00000; 01DB3300 (per-draw command builder) ~0.8 GB into VRAM CFE/CFF00000 + IO window 4DC/4DD00000; 01EA2100 ~0.33 GB IO 4D100000-4D300000; 01DCBC00 (Edge-style geometry) ~0.21 GB, the ONLY big writer the RSX reads as vertex arrays (C9C/C7E/C83/C7B00000). 01DBF980 / 01EA4600 never appear under their own EA: their results leave through the job runner into main memory. => chain: animation -> main heap -> 01DB3300 / PPU -> GPU data. NOT GPU-only data; a GPU-compute port would have to replace the animation jobs, the command builder and any PPU readers together -> not practical. Only clean candidate: 01DCBC00 (small CPU share). CONCLUSION: no worthwhile GPU offload left; next = real Deck test.
   MIN-SPEC CLOCK SWEEP mhz1 (2026-10-30, #267 build, FF + avx2 + GCM_FLIP_EVERY=2, SCSUM; user stepped the clock in matches; effective MHz logged per second via perf counters -> nhl\runs\mhz1.clock.csv / mhz1.aligned.csv): in-play [HZ] game counter by plateau: 1.38 GHz 3-4 | 1.51 4 | 1.68 3-6 | 2.41 5-6 | 2.63-2.74 4-7 | 3.10 5-7 | 3.40 5-7 | 3.71 6-8 | 5.3 (boost) 30 = full speed. USER: "slightly playable at 3.23 GHz, didn't get super awesome til 4+ GHz". => on 4C/8T (Zen 5 IPC) full speed needs ~4+ GHz (cliff between 3.7 and 5.3; not sampled). Caveat: the JM-waits metric is NOT comparable across runs/scenes (here 2.4 GHz gave ~160/s with game 5 vs deck9 ~111/s) -- use the in-play game counter + user feel. Steam Deck (Zen 2 @ 3.5 GHz ~ Zen 5 @ ~2.3 GHz) will be in the slow-motion range; 4-core PCs need a modern high-IPC 4+ GHz CPU. NEXT: same sweep on 6 cores (affinity FFF) -> min spec for the common 6-core CPUs.
   mhz2 (2026-10-30, same env; user capped with the power plan's PROCFREQMAX in MHz, logged effective clock): in-play game counter -- 3.98 GHz: 30 with short dips (one 2 s dip to 11-16) | 4.18: 30, one ~4 s dip to 9-12 | 4.37: 30, short dips to 14-21 | 4.74: solid 30, no dips. With mhz1's 3.71 GHz = 6-8 (slow motion): THE CLIFF IS BETWEEN 3.7 AND 4.0 GHz on 4C/8T (Zen 5 IPC). Min ~4.0 GHz (occasional dips), comfortable ~4.5+ GHz. Data: nhl\runs\mhz2.aligned.csv.
   mhz3 (2026-10-30, 6C/12T = affinity FFF, same env; auto knobs off at 6 cores): 1.77 GHz 4-5 | 2.36 5-6 | 2.86 mostly 5-7, one 8 s stretch ~30 | 3.40 mixed: 30 stretches with dips to 6-15 (borderline) | 3.98 solid 30 | 4.28 solid 30. => 6 cores move the line only a little (4C: cliff 3.7-4.0, dips up to 4.4; 6C: borderline at 3.4, solid from 4.0). So the limit is mostly the CRITICAL-PATH single-thread speed (the dependent job chain on the 3-SPU group + main), not total cores. MIN SPEC (Zen 5-class IPC): ~4.0 GHz with >= 4C/8T (6C gives a bit of margin: ~3.5-4.0). Older/lower-IPC CPUs need proportionally more. Data: nhl\runs\mhz3.aligned.csv.
   mhz4 (2026-10-30, 6C/12T, user set PROCFREQMAX 3600): the CPU actually ran 3.40 GHz (the cap snaps to the next lower P-state; 3600 is not reachable here -- steps seen: ~3.4, ~4.0, ~4.2-4.3). At 3.40 GHz: 16 s at 30, then ~35 s of slow motion (5-9) -> not reliable. => 6C/12T needs ~4.0 GHz Zen 5-class for solid play too; 6 cores only reduce the dips vs 4C. PUBLIC SPEC (put to the user): minimum = modern 6-core at ~4 GHz Zen 4/5-class single-thread (Ryzen 5 7600 / Core i5-12400 class); Ryzen 5000 (Zen 3, ~3.4 GHz Zen 5-equivalent) likely slow in heavy moments; recommended = Ryzen 7000/9000 or Intel 13th gen+ at 4.5 GHz+.
   mhz5 (2026-10-30, 6C/12T): cap 3600 -> ran 3.40 GHz again: ~45 s mixed (30 with dips to 5-14, several s each); cap 3800 -> ran 3.78 GHz: SOLID 30 for 35 s, no dips. => 6C/12T threshold between 3.40 and 3.78 GHz; 3.8 GHz (Zen 5-class) = solid. Final numbers: 6C min ~3.8 GHz, 4C min ~4.0 GHz (dips) / 4.7 solid.
   WHAT IS LEFT for 4C/8T @ 2.3 GHz (need ~1.7x total, i.e. cut ~40% of ~6.5 busy CPUs): (1) SPU codegen spills (~30% %rsp traffic in the hot 01DBF980/01EA4600 routines; lifter-level register allocation; biggest, hardest); (2) SPU channel/DMA runtime ~0.46 CPU (mfc_do_transfer, spu_overlay_note_get, spu_wrch, gcm_note_spu_put, spu_lookup, lockline); (3) PPU guest code 0.6 CPU (render thread mostly); (4) RSX ticker ~0.45 CPU (plan step 2: constants copy, FP hash memo, tex binds). Realistic: each 5-15%; full speed on this setup is not assured.
   STAND-IN SUMMARY (deck1-6): at 4C/8T + 2.3 GHz the match sim runs ~55-60% speed. #260 (AVX2 float) was the big fix (deck1 5-6 game/s -> deck2 30 at full clock). #261, #262 cap, poll back-off: each 0-12%. The CPU as a whole is full (~5.2 busy logical CPUs); no single thread limits. Caveat: 2.3 GHz Zen 5 ~ Deck Zen 2 @3.5 GHz is an IPC estimate; the real Deck (4 MB L3, LPDDR5, Proton) may land either side. RECOMMENDED next: one real Deck run before more stand-in tuning. Bigger levers if needed: SPU job code cost (spu1-3 real work 01DBF980 / 01EA4600, ~3.3 CPUs of SPU threads in total), a back-off for the 01E26780 job-runner idle poll, RSX ticker per-draw cost (plan step 2).

## >>> RELEASE CHECKLIST: FINAL PGO PASS (user asked to keep this, 2026-10-24) <<<
When everything else is fixed, before a release, redo PGO so the profile matches the final code:
 1. FREEZE the code (all fixes/optimisations in; nothing changes afterwards except the PGO build).
 2. Instrumented build: configure a folder with -DNHL_PGO_GEN=ON (see #156 / nhl\build_pgogen), full build.
 3. Profile BROADLY, several sessions, one .profraw each (LLVM_PROFILE_FILE=nhl\pgo\<name>.profraw; written every 30 s):
    boot + intro movies + menus; several matches with different teams/arenas; stoppages, replays, fights, shootouts; every game mode that matters. The instrumented exe runs slower -- expected.
 4. Merge all: llvm-profdata merge -o nhl\pgo\release.profdata nhl\pgo\*.profraw
 5. Release build with -DNHL_PGO_USE=...\release.profdata, verify with a normal speed match.
 5b. #257: the exe carries an AVX2 copy of the SPU code (renamed *_avx2 symbols, unprofiled until then) -- run at least one profiling session with NHL_SPU_ISA=avx2 so the Steam Deck path gets PGO too.
 6. Optional at that point: add ThinLTO on top (it did nothing alone, can add with PGO); build the AVX2 / Steam Deck variant from the SAME profile (counts are source-level, not CPU-specific).
 REFRESH 2026-10-31 (code = #280 + diagnostics #278/#279, #274 dormant): nhl\build_pgogen reconfigured (+NHL_PPU_V3, NHL_SPU_DUAL), instrumented exe 678 MB, smoke xpgo3 clean (1230 flips, profile writes OK). pgo1 (AVX-512, user: intro + menus + one full period with replay, stoppages, fight, icing, goal; no pregame movie; "painfully slow" = instrumentation): 0 errors, nhl\pgo\pgo1.profraw 2.5 GB. pgo2 (user: different team, pregame movie, entered a Winter Classic game (not played), shootout mode, menus): 0 errors, pgo2.profraw 2.7 GB. pgo3 (NHL_SPU_ISA=avx2 + RZ, ~8 min incl. a match): 0 errors, 1.0 GB. Merged -> nhl\pgo\release.profdata (67 MB, 4.43M blocks); nhl\build now NHL_PGO_USE=release.profdata (was nhl.profdata from 10-24), rebuilt: exe 426 MB, pre-refresh exe = NHLLegacy_280.exe. Smokes x281a (AVX-512) / x281b (AVX2 + RZ): 0 errors, 1511 / 1529 flips. Speed gain not measured yet (speed7 = speed6 setup would show it). Old pgo3 note: = NHL_SPU_ISA=avx2 match. Then merge -> nhl\pgo\release.profdata, nhl\build NHL_PGO_USE, rebuild, verify.
 TODO: a script (tools\pgo.ps1: build instrumented -> run profile session -> merge -> build optimised) so a refresh is mostly playing matches.

## >>> END-OF-PROJECT POLISH: USER BRIGHTNESS / GAMMA (user asked, 2026-10-27) <<<
Do this near the end (before the PGO freeze above). Monitor-fit brightness, like PC games' "adjust until barely visible".
 1. NEVER touch the #208 framebuffer sRGB conversion (it is the game's real transfer function; blending, tonemap and post-effects depend on it).
 2. Apply a display-only gamma/brightness as ONE final pass at present (backbuffer -> swapchain), default 1.0 = identical to PS3/RPCS3. Setting in the port's settings file + NHL_GAMMA env for testing. Cheap (one fullscreen pass; check Steam Deck cost).
 3. Later, if the port gets its own settings overlay: a calibration screen ("adjust until the logo is barely visible"). NHL's own options menu has no brightness setting to hook.

## >>> END-OF-PROJECT TODO: ICE BOUNCE LIGHT ON PLAYERS (user asked, 2026-10-27) <<<
User: "I honestly think it looks more accurate with the ice reflection off" -- deferred to the end.
 - What it is: a 512x512 DXT5 top-down RINK image (gamma RGB) that the player/gear programs project by world XZ as bounce light from the ice (jersey program: unit 9; others u1/u3/u4). F3 mode 4 (#216, NHL_LIVEKEYS=1) blanks it on player draws -> the user's preferred look (play318).
 - Already ruled out: shader code (== RPCS3 FP225 incl. h8.xy = k74), address mode (WRAP), gamma, mips. Open question: are the constants (k74 rink scale, k85-k100 strength / height falloff) or the VS world position different from RPCS3, or is it authentic and just disliked?
 - Data waiting: play319 (FPK_REGS=all for the 9 u9 programs + their HLSL in fpdump) -- analyse when this is picked up. COLLECTED 2026-10-27: 60 [FPKKEY] lines in nhl\runs\play319.err.log, 25 fresh fpdump HLSL files, 4 F9 captures (user close-up of the Canes black jersey) in nhl\runs\play319_cap, 0 errors.
 - Options then: (a) fix a constant if one is wrong; (b) a port setting (settings file + env) "ice bounce light off", like #216 mode 4 but matched to that texture's role only, default chosen by the user.
 - ANALYSIS 2026-10-27 (user: "let's still fix it, disabling is my personal choice"): play319 logged CB11C776 (39), 85641E43 (19), 6B00D51A (2). CB11C776 (rink = u4): uv = world xz * k25 (0.0163 = 1/61 m rink length) + 0.5; height falloff saturate(k61.x - (y - k24.z 0.75) / k60.y 1); strength = tc4.w * falloff * k62.w (2) * albedo, added. tc4.w from VS 0x30E146A5 (vpdump slot49) = projected light cone: position through one of TWO per-draw projector matrices (c160-c163 / c164-c167, c168/c169) picked by cc0 = tc0.u < c467.y (kit-texture half), then N.L * radial falloff saturate(1 - (x^2 + y^2)) * depth gate 0 <= z <= 1. Compared with RPCS3 VertexProgram137/213/74: identical incl. the conditional polarity (equal(cc0, 0)) and the gate/falloff ops; constant values consistent. => VS + FS + texture state all match; only the per-draw projector matrices / vertex data are unchecked (same game code feeds RPCS3).
 - EVIDENCE IT MAY BE AUTHENTIC: play319 frame_50 (bounce on) shows a grey-teal sheen on the black jersey's shoulders/upper chest; the user's XBOX reference (Canes black, 2026-10-27, third image) shows the same sheen. Mode 4 (off) = flat black. Need an RPCS3 shot of the same black jersey to decide.
 - DECIDED 2026-10-27 (user's 4 RPCS3 shots, Canes black, Slavin 74): the grey-teal shoulder YOKE is the jersey design; RPCS3 shows it clearly teal = ours with bounce ON (play319); with bounce OFF (play318 mode 4) the yoke sinks to near-black. => THE ICE BOUNCE IS AUTHENTIC, not a bug. The off switch stays on this list purely as the user's style preference (option b).
 - Side note: RPCS3 shots show fine white SPECKLES over all dark kit (jersey, pants, gloves); ours and the user's Xbox Canes shot have none -> most likely an RPCS3 artifact; not pursued.
 - play320 (normal render re-check, user drove): USER "I guess it is correct" -> jerseys/socks CLOSED as correct; bounce-off remains only as the optional style setting above.

  * PLAY NOW TILE DATE (user 2026-10-28: main-menu tile "Capitals vs Hurricanes 12/31/2015"; RPCS3 shows today's month/day in the 2015-16 season). Game reads "today" through libc time() = func_016D2F30 -> syscall 145 sys_time_get_current_time (cellRtcGetCurrentClockLocalTime is only used once, func_00AC5F8C, for the UTC offset). CAUSE: runtime/syscalls/sys_timer.c on Windows returned the QPC UPTIME as the seconds since 1970 -> early Jan 1970 UTC = 12/31/1969 in US time zones -> the game mapped 12/31 into its season. #246: GetSystemTimePreciseAsFileTime wall clock (like the POSIX CLOCK_REALTIME branch and RPCS3). Built, smoke x246 clean, deployed (old exe NHLLegacy_245.exe in build\ and install_test\). Also fixes 1970 timestamps inside saves. NOT yet seen by the user: expect the tile to show 10/28/2015-style dates (today's month/day in 2015-16, like RPCS3); off-season months behave like RPCS3.

  * 60 Hz GAMEPLAY -- CODE STUDY (2026-10-28, user asked for a 60 Hz setting; step 1 = static study, no runs):
    - Game delta time: func_0140378C (main thread, per frame). dt = (now_ns - last_ns, kept at obj+0x580) * 6e-8 = 60/1e9 -> dt in 1/60 s UNITS (30 fps = 2.0, 60 fps = 1.0); clamped to 5.0; averaged over the last 10 frames (x0.1); x0.5 x (1/30) -> seconds -> func_00C1F530 (sets the sim's dt). Constants at 0x01403768: 30.0, 0, 6e-8, 5.0 (max), 2.0 (FIXED dt fallback = a 30 Hz step, substituted in some branches: obj+0xDC list check, measured dt > 1.0 with byte obj+0x589), 1.0, 1.0 (ms sleep), 0.1, 0.5, 1/30. => the sim is TIME-BASED in 60 Hz units: a faster loop should not speed up gameplay (unless the fixed-2.0 branch is the one taken in matches -- unverified).
    - The same function's 1 ms sleep loop at lr 0x01403968 (<= 500 x) waits for global byte 0x024DAD84 to leave 1 (set by main, cleared in subsystems 0x014F4AD4 / 0x01519128 / 0x01548018); it is the dominant wait in the pre-game intro (play257 ~700 sleeps/s), not in live play.

If main reaches ~60/s and the period clock still runs at real time -> pacing is vblank-based and a proper 60 Hz option is a pacing tweak; if main stays 30 -> the cap is the cond var / frame-due logic (more tracing).     - FRAME PACING TRACED (2026-10-28, static + live title-run console reads): top-level main tick func_000148F0 runs N game-frame steps, N = frame scheduler *0x0227C3D4 (heap 0x4D02A1E0 in every run) field +0xC, filled each tick by func_014C8078 = pacer->vtbl[2]() (pacer at sched+0x10, vtable 0x01D72C68; slot2 func_014C35AC = +0x10 - +0xC - 1 under futex +0x20; slot5 func_01A26A5C -> func_014C33CC per step; slot3 func_014C2E20 reset). N=0 -> 1 ms usleep @0x00014CD4. Pacer = an 11-slot frame-pipeline tracker (+0x8 counter array, +0x10 = 11, +0xC = 10, +0x14 = 1 participant, +0x18 phase 0..10 changes). Main's per-frame cond_wait @0x00C78544 is the Job Manager run-and-wait helper func_00C7849C (callers func_00C785C8 / func_00C7B3CC), not the pacing. Who advances the pipeline phase (render/flip side?) is still open.

## STUCK STARTING LINEUP -- ROOT CAUSE FOUND (2026-10-28), fix #231 built
- Not "a new team": it was the SECOND match of the session. PPU thread slots leaked (flags ignored + exit/epilogue state bug), 64 slots full after the first match, the MoviePlayer2 thread for the lineup -> match transition silently failed to create. Evidence + fix in LOCAL_PATCHES #231. Deployed to install_test (old exe NHLLegacy_230.exe).
- NEXT USER RUN: play one match (or part, then quit to the menu), then start a SECOND match in the same session -> lineup must reach Press START. Log check: no "thread_create FAILED", tids reused, max tid stays low; saves still work (auto_load/save threads are joinable, flags=1).
- USER RUN #231 (game_20261028_000845, 2026-10-28, "played through a couple teams, the game ended up hanging"): no create failures (64 creates, max tid 25 = slots reused), but at t=235 main-heap corruption (NULL free-list links in the allocator, tid 23) -> hang at t=237. Suspect: thread-ID reuse. #231b = unique IDs (see LOCAL_PATCHES). Old exe NHLLegacy_231.exe. Deployed to install_test.

- LAUNCHER BUG (2026-10-28 00:23): install_test\nhl_settings.ini was rewritten with ONLY [video] (fullscreen = 1) -> launcher asked for a reinstall. Two NHLLauncher.exe instances were running (pids 3404, 31136): save_video() = load + set + save; a failed/partial load (other instance mid-write) saved an empty ini + [video]. Install data was intact; ini restored by hand (fullscreen = 1). FIX built in nhl\build_launcher (00:33): save_video refuses to save when the existing file cannot be read; Ini::save writes <file>.tmp + MoveFileExW. Copied to install_test 00:3x after the user closed both windows. Also refresh dist + install_iso launchers at release. Possible follow-up: single-instance launcher (named mutex).

  * REAL CAUSE (2026-10-28, offline proof): func_016040E0 = EA's RefPack decoder. The source sits INSIDE the output buffer (buffer = unpacked size 0x555D4). If dst_end lies inside the source it takes an in-place branch (last 4 KB of source saved to the stack); otherwise it decodes plainly with NO overlap protection. Mod logo BIGFs have entry "88" (RefPack) followed by padding + sg2 + a 32-byte entry, so the stream ends short of the buffer end -> plain decode -> the writer overtakes unread input near the end. Scratchpad inplace_sim.py (replays the game's decode): gap 0 -> 35/35 correct; gap 3/35/259 -> 35/35 WRONG from ~20 bytes before the end, then a garbage-command runaway (the 24-28 byte overrun of #236; varies by data). Same in RPCS3 presumably (harmless heap neighbour there).

  * In transition frames the game resolves the MSAA scene 0x02980000 (two NV3089 strips 1023+257, AA factor 2) straight into the DISPLAY buffer it is about to show -- triple-buffered rotation 0x00790000 / 0x00010000 / 0x003D0000 -- then the bright pass (fp 0x0FDFAAC1) and the composite (fp 0x0295A381) read that display buffer; normal frames resolve into 0x047D0000 instead (frame 68 on).
  * Our SRV pass binds ANY display-buffer read to s_screen_copy (SCREEN_AS_TEX = previous presented frame) before looking at off_rt_find, so the blit's RT (offrt[31..33]) was never sampled: composite output + its bloom fed back every frame = runaway white bloom; the new shot never reached the screen.
  * Ruled out on the way: the skipped "[GPUBLIT] skip: scale 200000/F286B" (= 0x043F0000 -> 640x760, dt = 720/760: the player-card mask path, not this); X8 display alpha (display surface fmt 0x...48 = A8R8G8B8, alpha is real).

  * SPU CODEGEN LOOK (2026-10-26, no code change): build = NHL_SPU_AVX512 ON (lean #142 float path; the AVX2/Deck path is the heavier spu__ax_* exact path). 01EA4600 func B668 hot loop (~110 SPU instrs, 30 LS loads, ~32 fm/fma, shufb/selb, 8 stores; register-promoted) compiles to 811 x86 instrs/iteration (llvm-objdump on nhl\build\NHLLegacy.exe at the map address; scratchpad b668.asm): ~283 vector moves incl. 164 stack refs (spills: ~70 live SPU regs vs 32 xmm), ~180 for the per-op exactness guards (vandps + vpcmpud->k + kortestb + jne + vaddps POS0 per float op), ~38 LS address masks + vpshufb byte swaps. Hottest function = only 6-8% of SPU time, the rest spread wide => function-by-function tuning ~10-25% total; the full-crowd shot needs ~5-10x. Options for the user: (a) incremental codegen, (b) opt-in approximate SPU floats like RPCS3's default (drop guards: ~20-30%, risk subtle differences), (c) first check whether RPCS3 also dips in that shot.

play227 (NHL_EVRING=1, saved to nhl\runs\play227_evring.txt): all 8 dumps hit the 640x760 number composites (fp 0x0F38FD81) before any 0x0F399B01 draw -- raise the dump limit / dump per distinct fp next time. In the 200 events before a number-composite draw: 86 SET_SHADER_PROGRAM, 2 NV3089 blits, 112 inline-transfer words ALL into local 0x0FFFF000 (a 128-byte scratch block, cf. play156/157 notes) and NONE into the bound program.
LEADING HYPOTHESIS: the game writes each patched program (or its constants) into the scratch block at local 0x0FFFF000 with NV308A inline transfers, then COPIES that block to the program's location with a transfer we do not emulate or emulate wrongly (NV0039 memory-to-memory? NV3089 with a different destination/format? cellGcmSetTransferData?) -- so the bound programs keep their zero constants.
NEXT: (1) read play227_evring.txt: the 'I' words written to 0xCFFFF000.. (do they look like FP ucode/constants? which floats?) and what follows them (B events: src 0xCFFFF000 -> dst?). (3) Compare the scratch words with the RPCS3 shader cache constants for the crowd (k8 = 0.507,0.234,0.204,0.65 etc., see crowd notes). Then fix the transfer, re-test helmets/gloves/pads/crowd, and re-check the tiled-number jersey glitch (#169 interaction).

Present path itself looks right (one Present per batch on the swap chain's current buffer).
- #112b: CAP_HOT_N=<n> (F9 burst length, default 4, max 200) + CAP_BB_ONLY=1 (backbuffer + manifest only, fast). Smoke bb0 OK. play161 = CAP_HOT_N=40 CAP_BB_ONLY=1: the user presses F9 while it is blinking -> 40 consecutive presented frames; look for outlier frames (brightness/diff) and compare their manifests with the neighbours.
- play161 RESULT (F9 burst while blinking; window closed after 5 frames): all 5 backbuffers PURE WHITE (mean 255). Batches MISALIGNED: draws 776 / 1187 / 760 / 1073 / 749 -- some presents hold two scene chains (two CLEAR 0x02980000), others half of one. Their "display" draws (rt=0) start with fp 0x0F390801, vp 1024x1024, 3500-vertex meshes, ps=0, surf=0x0 = the SHADOW-MAP pass (depth only).
- ROOT CAUSE (strong): current_rt_off() returns the colour offset, and a colour-less surface has offset 0 = the backend's code for THE DISPLAY. So shadow-pass CLEARS count as display clears -> presents fire mid-frame + s_d3d.clear_color is re-set from them (white) -> a batch that ends before the scene composite shows a flat white screen = the constant blinking; the shadow draws themselves land on the backbuffer (cmask 0, invisible).
- #113: rsx_depth_only_pass(): with SET_SURFACE_COLOR_TARGET = NONE (color_target_set && color_target == 0) d3d12_draw_arrays / d3d12_draw_indexed / d3d12_clear drop the work ([DEPTHONLY] counts per 5 s); RSX_DEPTHONLY_KEEP=1 restores. Depth-only output is unused here anyway (depth-as-texture not emulated). Title smoke do1: renders normally (no depth-only pass at the title).
  - UNVERIFIED that NHL's shadow surface really has color_target 0 (it could instead be target A with colour offset 0) -- play162 logs RT_OFFDBG to check; if [DEPTHONLY] stays silent, key colour offset 0 (non-display) as its own offscreen RT instead.

But the 40-frame burst still alternates bright (mean ~190-206, correct image) and dark (24-44: the ICE black except a few rectangular tiles) in runs.

User: "1 white flash but better than before, players and their equipment are still flashing".

FIX #114: dr_note_view() (called by all 3 VP record sites) sets dr->is_blit = 0. Smoke bl0: title OK. play164 = normal rendering, user checks player flashing (F9 optional).

- CAUSE (strong): the render_frame pre-pass CPU-writes shared per-frame state while the previous frame may still execute on the GPU (FRAME_COUNT 2, move_to_next_frame only waits for the frame before last): per-draw SRV windows DRAW_SRV_BASE + d*16 (ONE set, no parity, unlike vp_cb/vp_vb) and texture slots (a slot unused this frame is re-filled with another texture, staging rewritten). The GPU then samples the next frame's descriptors/texels.

- Two descriptor/RT bugs found (offline):
  1. MAX_OFF_RTS = 16 but the match uses ~30 colour surfaces ([RTOFF] play162: jersey/number composites 640x760, 512x512, 1024x512 at many offsets). off_rt_get evicts a slot "not used this frame" (used = only rendered-into this frame) -> a jersey rendered once and then only sampled loses its contents.
  2. vp_upload_tex_slot wrote its SRV at heap[1 + slot]; since #100 (1024 slots) uploads into slot 4..19 overwrote offscreen-RT SRVs (RT_SRV_BASE 5..20) and slots >= 31 overwrote per-draw windows (DRAW_SRV_BASE 32 + d*16) already filled earlier in the same pre-pass. Nothing reads heap[1+slot] for VP draws.
- #116: MAX_OFF_RTS 64; DRAW_SRV_BASE = RT_SRV_BASE + MAX_OFF_RTS + 3 (72); slot SRVs at SLOT_SRV_BASE = DRAW_SRV_BASE + MAX_DRAWS*16; SRV_HEAP_SIZE = SLOT_SRV_BASE + VP_TEX_SLOTS. Smoke srv0: title renders correctly. play166 = normal rendering; user checks jersey flashing + white flash.
- play166 RESULT (#116): user "flashing jerseys slightly better, still white flash". Jersey flashes through "white, black, purple ... just about everything"; the white flash comes when a SHOT is taken (either team). Frame median 93 ms (short sample; play165 78 ms).
  - Player draws (VS 4478E6DE / 90BF956C) bind ~60 distinct textures and NONE equals an offscreen-RT base (play164 manifests vs play162 [RTOFF] list): jerseys are plain guest-memory textures, not RT composites.
  - Hypothesis: TEXTURE-POOL RACE. We write fence labels when the FIFO is processed but upload textures only at render_frame (frame boundary). If NHL refills a jersey/number texture pool once the GPU reports done, we upload the next occupant / half-written data.
- #117 (diag): TEXRACE_VS=<vs hashes>: units 0-3 checksummed at record time (vp_record_cb) and again in the pre-pass; [TEXRACE] logs changes + a 5 s summary. play167 = TEXRACE_VS=4478E6DE,90BF956C; user plays ~30 s incl. a few shots.

Upload path checked by reading: per-slot staging buffers, correct PSR<->COPY_DEST barriers, null SRV for unresolved units -- nothing obvious.
- #118 (diag): [SLOTMISMATCH] (pre-pass: returned slot does not hold the requested texture), [SLOTSTALE] (draw time: a slot this draw's descriptor uses was re-given to another texture), manifest bnd=<kind><id> per unit 0-3 (R = RT index, S = texture slot, C = screen copy, - = null/none). play168 = CAP_HOT_N=20 CAP_BB_ONLY=1; user presses F9 when a jersey flickers.

- NEW EXPLANATION: textures were uploaded with MipLevels = 1 (level 0 only). Jerseys are detailed (stripes/crests/numbers), players ~40 px tall -> each pixel point-samples one texel of a huge texture -> sparkle through every colour as players move; plain pants/socks look stable. Samplers are already MIN_MAG_MIP_LINEAR.
- #119: vp_upload_tex_slot uploads the guest mip chain (levels packed back to back after level 0 for swizzled/DXT 2D textures; BC levels down to 4x4; each level decoded into a 512-aligned staging slice and copied to its subresource), VPTexSlot.nl, SRVs expose all levels (srv_write_ex MipLevels -1). TEX_NO_MIPS=1 restores level 0 only. Smoke mip0: title renders correctly, no GPU errors. play169 = normal rendering; user checks jersey sparkle + white flash.
- play169 RESULT (#119 mips): user "jersey still flash, white still flash" -> mips NOT the cause (kept: correct). No GPU errors; median 93 ms.

So the CD8F10A4 analysis was the wrong shader; the screen is painted by another FP (the ice). #93 confirmed applied (vsdump A427F53E has o[1]=1).

- RINK SHADER = FP key 0x220C075E (normal) / 0x721DD75E (id mode); same on-screen lavender in play109 and play120 => same id colour 939CF0. fpdump fp_rt02980000_0FE12D81_220C075E.hlsl: out = tex1(tc0.xy) * (tex2(tc0.zw).rgb * tex2.a * k3.x * k6 * k11.x + saturate(shadow terms)). Unit 2 = LIGHTMAP (DXT5 256x256, e.g. 0x07065000/0x07093000), units 0/3 = 4x4 LN A8R8G8B8 dummy 0x0147D000 sampled at tc1/w, tc2/w (0/0 with the zero shadow matrices). Black if k3/k6/k11 = 0, the lightmap reads black, or tc0.zw wrong (VS D49BCA3A sets o[7].zw = c467.x = 0!).
- #96 FPK_KEY=<hex key>: fp_k[0..15] + t2 of that program's draws at exec time. play122 = FPK_KEY=220C075E + VTXDUMP_RT (300/30 s) + VP_DUMP_ALL.

VS tc0.zw sources: lightmapped meshes use a9 (5E0C6C90, E38195D1, ...), single-UV meshes use c467.x (= 0) -> lightmap texel (0,0). So black <=> the lightmap sample is black / alpha 0.
