## >>> (superseded) scene BLACK because the game uploads ZERO light matrices <<<
- Chain fixed this session:
  - #85 GPU resolve blit 0x02980000 -> 0x047D0000;
  - #87 Apt slot filled from RT 0x043F0000. The match screen went WHITE -> BLACK (user, play89), with occasional white flashes (frames whose placeholder lands in a batch without the scene).
- The scene geometry is CORRECT: NHL_FP_SOLID magenta covered 99.6% of 0x02980000 and the user saw the player-outline circles (play90).
- Not the cause: depth (DEPTH_OFF made no difference); buffered FP constants (NHL_FP_BAKED=1 still black, play92); textures/formats (scene units 0-3 bound, units 4+ are game-disabled stale bindings; fmt 0x94 X16 is allowed); vertex inputs (VTXDUMP_RT: positions, a3 colour ~0.4, a8 texcoords all sane); VS translation (vsdump: correct); per-draw constant copy (full 512).
- ROOT: in every scene draw the vertex constants c24..c28 and c32 (the light/shadow projection matrices feeding tc1/tc2, i.e. the lighting texture lookups) are ALL ZERO. TCONST_HIST shows the game WRITES slots 0-83 every frame, so it uploads zeros: the light/shadow setup is never computed (c0-c7 camera, c36-c39 world and c64/c65 texcoord values are sane).
- Typical scene FP (fpdump fp_rt02980000_0FE08001_*): out = (saturate(tex0(tc2/w)*a + tex2(tc3/w)*b) + fp_constants[9].x) * tex1(tc0).
- NEXT: find the guest code that computes/uploads c24..c31.
  - (a) Capture the FIFO words around SET_TRANSFORM_CONSTANT_LOAD=24 in a gameplay frame (address + where the command-buffer bytes were written from: PPU_WWATCH on that IO address -> writer function).
  - (b) From the writer, follow the source matrix (likely a light/shadow-camera object; maybe filled by an SPU job, or from an environment/lighting data file).
  - (c) Compare with RPCS3 if possible.
- Also open: depth-only passes (surf=0 ct=0: the 1024x1024 shadow map, the depth pre-pass) are recorded as rt=0 (display) and write the display's depth buffer. They need their own zeta-keyed depth targets, and the shadow map needs depth-as-texture. The zeta resolves 0x03120000 -> 0x00B70000/0x00F30000 are not handled.
- Also: occasional white flash frames (#87 only fires when the placeholder and the scene RT are in one batch).

## >>> MILESTONE 10 (2026-10-20, play69): PAST THE HEXAGONS -- in-game HUD on screen <<<
- ROOT CAUSE of the frozen EA SPORTS transition: the D3D12 backend's draw-record batch (MAX_DRAWS 2048) overflowed on gameplay frames. NHL presents only at the clear boundary, and only once a batch holds a display-buffer draw. Gameplay's offscreen passes filled the batch, every later draw (the display composite too) was dropped, and the batch could never present or empty, so the window kept the last transition frame while the game ran (play68: draws=2048 presented=0 nodisp ~100/s). Fixed by LOCAL_PATCHES #77 (4096 + flush-when-full). Diagnostics #74/#75 (RSX_FPRINT, RSX_PRESENTDBG) found it.
- All the SPU work this session (#69-#73) was real but NOT the blocker.
- play69 (user screenshot): the HUD shows (EA SPORTS scoreboard NSH 0 CAR 0, 1st 18:29, "Faceoff" banner), but:
  1. The 3D scene renders WHITE. The HUD composites fine. Suspects: HDR tonemap/auto-exposure (a 1x1 luminance RTT read as 0 -> blown to white), the 4x-MSAA HDR RT 0x02980000 (fmt 0x3148, AA ignored by the backend), depth-as-texture (zeta 0xB70000/0xF30000 sampled as ARGB8, unemulated).
  2. Gameplay crawls: the guest flips ~0.5/s (fc +1 per present, every ~2 s) and the render thread calls DumpGraphicsError repeatedly (its GPU-fence timeout handler). The audio stays smooth. The bottleneck is the RSX/D3D12 host thread (FIFO walk + render_frame); first suspect is per-draw GPU syncs/readbacks or CPU vertex processing on ~600-900 draws (+ ~3.4M constant words per batch).
  3. One FIFO resync (jump-park at io 0x008C2D00) at the very end.
- NEXT: profile the RSX host thread in gameplay (tools\prof_hot.ps1 / PERF=1), then fix the white scene.

## >>> SESSION 3 (2026-10-19/20): play63 SPU profile analysed <<<
- #69 worked: the out-of-line float helpers fell from ~45% to ~1% of SPU time. SPU time is now ~79% in program 0x01EA4600's own lifted code (func_000026F8 inclusive), plus the runtime per-dispatch hooks spu_task_launch_check (8.7%) and yz_lockstep_tick (2.6%). Main is still ~3 fps.
- CODEGEN AUDIT of the hottest loop, nhl_prog_01EA4600_spu_func_0000B668 (llvm-objdump of build\CMakeFiles\NHLLegacy.dir\spu\nhl_prog_01EA4600\spu_recomp.c.obj):
  - One loop iteration is ~2,970 x86 instructions for ~120 SPU instructions (~25:1). The gpr file stays in memory (ctx->gpr), which is expected.
  - About half is the bit-exact float emulation now INLINED in spu_helpers.h (#69 AVX2 path): fm/fma = widen to double, TwoSum, truncate, then range checks. That's ~35-45 instructions each (vcvtps2pd 83, vsubpd 96, vpcmpgtq 56, vcmpeqpd 56, vextractf128 64 per iteration).
  - Every spu_ls_read128/write128 also runs ~5 debug-probe checks (g_spu_ls_probe, image_id==2 && policy_mode [another game's WWS probe], g_spu_ls_watch_n, g_spu_smc_watch). They are well predicted but ~14 instructions per load.
  - shufb is ~20 instructions (3 word-swap pshufbs plus special-selector masks).
- PROPOSED FIX (awaiting the user's OK):
  (1) AVX-512 fast path (Zen 5 has AVX512F/DQ/VL): vfmadd/vmul/vadd with embedded round-toward-zero ({rz-sae}). For normal inputs and a normal result this is bit-exact with SPU truncating single-precision. vfpclassps flags denormal/inf/NaN inputs, denormal or FLT_MAX results and non-trivial zero results, and only those lanes take the existing exact path. ~8 instructions vs ~40. Verify by extending tools\test_spu_float_avx2.c (random + edge cases vs the scalar reference).
  (2) Compile the LS debug probes out of the NHL build (a macro, default on upstream).
  (3) Make the per-dispatch hooks cheap: an inline flag check before calling spu_task_launch_check / yz_lockstep_tick.

## Current blocker: match start freezes on the loading screen ("Loading" never becomes the Starting Lineup / Press START)

Symptoms: the loading UI stops animating, music keeps playing, title-bar FPS jumps from ~15–30 to ~165.

### Fixed this session
1. **Save-data HLE scratch inside NHL's BSS** (LOCAL_PATCHES #47).
   - `libs/system/cellSaveData.c` used guest scratch at 0x024E0000; NHL's data segment is 0x020E0000..0x0252DE00.
   - Every save op (`cellSaveDataListAutoLoad` after PRESS START) zeroed 0x024E0000+.
   - That wiped the "AI" entry of the game's named-handler list (node 0x024E074C), causing a NULL call at the match start.
   - Fix: `boot_main.cpp` moves it to 0xBF100000 (and cellGame's game-data scratch to 0xBF120000) via `cellSaveData_set_scratch_region` / new `cellGame_set_scratch_region`.
   - Verified in play13: the node stays intact. The freeze persisted for reason 2.
2. **Swap interval 1 runs unpaced** (LOCAL_PATCHES #48). The game's swap-interval routine is `func_00CBDD10`.
   - The front end uses interval 2, paced by the label 0x4B0 countdown.
   - At match start it switches to interval 1 (two `cellGcmSetFlipMode(2)` calls from 0xCBDD7C / 0xCBDDDC). From then on it relies on the RSX holding each flip until vblank.
   - We flipped instantly, so it ran at ~165 fps, vblank-based frame time came out ~0, and game time stopped.
   - Fix in `libs/video/cellGcmSys.c`: in VSYNC mode at most one FIFO flip per vblank. The walker parks on the flip word, `cellGcmTickVBlank` clears the gate, and the stuck detector ignores the hold. `GCM_VSYNC_FLIP=0` turns it off.
   - play17 `[gcmstat]` showed the hold worked (~110 holds/s) but `catchup=105/s`. NHL keeps ~128 KB queued, beyond the general 64 KB catch-up exception, so most flips still went through.
   - **LAST CHANGE (built, NOT yet tested):** VSYNC mode now uses its own catch-up threshold, `GCM_VSYNC_CATCHUP`, default 4 MB.

### Next test
```
powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools\run_nhl.ps1 -Seconds 7200 -Tag play18 -MonitorSec 30 -Env @{ GCM_STAT='1'; NHL_VP6_BRIDGE='0' }
```
- Play to the match.
- In `nhl\runs\play18.err.log`, the `[gcmstat]` lines should show `fifo_flips` ≤ 60 and `catchup=0` after the switch.
- Hoped-for result: the Starting Lineup appears and the match runs at ≤ 60 fps.
- If the game deadlocks instead (the ring fills and its buffer callback can't get space while we hold): try `GCM_VSYNC_CATCHUP` = a bit under the ring size, or hold in the ticker instead.

### Other open items
- **The load hang at the socks (play2/play3) may be a timing race.** With the 7 in-game-only jobs (01DE9800, 01E03480, 01E04080, 01E04C80, 01E15180, 01E1FE00, 01E21880) interpreted or traced, the load completed. Lockstep (traced) found no real divergence; all reports were bi/bisl artefacts of partial tracing. Watch for a recurrence.
- **Resident SPU code slots** 8 → 32 (`spu_context.h`); eviction skips the running code. "All 32 slots taken" still appears in-game, so slots may go stale (code loaded by a DMA list, not noted?). Worth a look.
- **VP6 bridge:** with `NHL_VP6_BRIDGE=0` the guest decodes the movies itself fine now, and loading runs at 30 fps instead of 15. Consider making bridge-off the default (run_nhl.ps1).
- **Team-colour panels** on the matchup bar overhang the angled bar ends: a mask/stencil clipping issue (cf. #33).
- **`barrier_watch_hit`** costs 5–11% exclusive CPU on the render thread even with PPU_WWATCH unset (vm_write32/64 call it every store). Cheap FPS win.
- **The user's PC BSODs** (0x1A MEMORY_MANAGEMENT 0x404; earlier 0x1E/0x3B/0x7E/0x0A) point to RAM instability (EXPO/XMP). Suggested: disable EXPO and run a memory test.
- Phase C (Vulkan backend) has not started.

### New diagnostics added this session (all env-gated)
- `GCM_STAT=1`: `[gcmstat]` once a second (flip paths, holds, catch-up bypasses, backlog, label 0x4B0).
- `GCM_DRAINTL=<lines>` / `GCM_DRAINTL_AFTER` / `GCM_DRAINTL_ARM=1`: the RSX timeline (`[gtl]`). Helper: scratchpad tlmerge.py.
- `RSX_FRAMESTAT=1`: per-second render targets of the presented frame.
- `PS3_HLE_HIST=<s>`: HLE call histogram by (name, tid, lr).
- `PS3_SCTIME` (+ `PS3_SCTIME_ARM=<path part>`, `PS3_SCTIME_MAX`), `GCM_ARM_FLIPMODE=1`: arm the traces at a file open or at the late SetFlipMode.
- `SPU_WWATCH=<ea>`: SPU PUTs that change a word. `NHL_WORDWATCH=<ea>`: a 1 ms sampler. `PPU_WW_GUARD=1`: page guard (found the save-data writer).
- `PS3_COND_TRACE_MAX`; the debug console `spus` command (live SPU pc/lr/chan-wait).
- run_nhl.ps1: `-MonitorSec <n>` (stat/waits/spus every n s, threads every 4th), `-MonitorProf`, `-DbgExtra`.
- `NHL_SPU_TRACE_ONLY=<ea,...>` in nhl_spu_lift.py (lockstep only some jobs).
- `GCM_FLIP_OWN_CB=0` (Phase B flip-marker A/B).
